1	Yes, Rune is specifically designed to generate trajectories from open source code repositories. The d2l_mining.py module provides the infrastructure for mining GitHub repositories to create the trajectory data needed for LoRA training d2l_mining.py:1-5 .
2	
3	GitHub Mining Functions
4	PR Diff Chains
5	The mine_pr_diff_chains() function extracts iterative coding sessions from pull requests d2l_mining.py:17-47 :
6	
7	Captures the sequence: initial commit → review comments → revision commits
8	Produces trajectory records with task_id, task_description (PR title + body), steps (chronological commit diffs), and outcome ("merged" or "closed")
9	Issue-Commit Chains
10	The mine_issue_commit_chains() function links GitHub issues to their fixing commits d2l_mining.py:50-79 :
11	
12	Scans commit messages for "fixes #N", "closes #N", or "resolves #N" patterns
13	Groups linked commits as trajectory steps
14	Returns trajectories with issue descriptions as task_description and commit sequence as steps
15	Why This Data Source Works
16	This approach captures the three structural properties that make trajectories valuable for training background.md:221-240 :
17	
18	Temporal ordering - Commits and reviews have a clear causal sequence
19	Procedural abstraction - PRs and issues encode how problems are solved step-by-step
20	Self-correcting structure - Review comments and bug fixes naturally contain failure-recovery patterns
21	Integration with Training Pipeline
22	The mined trajectories follow the same structure as agent-generated trajectories, with fields that match what format_for_distillation() expects d2l_data.py:21-24 :
23	
24	task_description - from PR title/body or issue text
25	steps - sequence of code changes and reviews
26	outcome - "merged"/"closed" for PRs, "closed"/"open" for issues
27	Current Status
28	Both mining functions are currently stubs that raise NotImplementedError d2l_mining.py:45-48 . They're designed to run on an L4 VM with network access and a GitHub token to avoid rate limiting d2l_mining.py:3-4 .
29	
30	Notes
31	The mining functions are designed for high-bandwidth data collection on dedicated training infrastructure, not for CI environments
32	Both functions require a github_token parameter for authentication and rate limit avoidance
33	The trajectory format produced by mining is compatible with the existing distillation pipeline in d2l_prep.py d2l_prep.py:47-84
```

1	"""GitHub trajectory mining for coding session distillation.
2	
3	Designed to run on an L4 VM with network access, not in CI.
4	Stubs only — implementation runs on the training VM.
5	"""
6	
7	from __future__ import annotations
8	
9	import logging
10	from typing import Any
11	
12	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
13	
14	__all__ = ["mine_pr_diff_chains", "mine_issue_commit_chains"]
15	
16	
17	def mine_pr_diff_chains(
18	    repo: str,
19	    max_prs: int = 100,
20	    github_token: str | None = None,
21	) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
22	    """Extract PR diff chains from a GitHub repository.
23	
24	    Each chain represents an iterative coding session: initial commit ->
25	    review comments -> revision commits. The resulting trajectory records
26	    capture the back-and-forth of code review as a multi-step improvement
27	    process, suitable for distillation.
28	
29	    Returns trajectory dicts with the following fields:
30	    - task_id: f"pr_{repo}_{pr_number}" (e.g., "pr_owner/repo_42")
31	    - task_description: PR title concatenated with body text
32	    - steps: list of commit diffs in chronological order
33	    - outcome: "merged" or "closed" depending on PR final state
34	
35	    Args:
36	        repo: GitHub repository in "owner/repo" format (e.g., "python/cpython").
37	        max_prs: Maximum number of PRs to process. Defaults to 100.
38	        github_token: Personal access token for GitHub API authentication.
39	            Required for private repos and to avoid rate limiting.
40	
41	    Returns:
42	        List of trajectory dicts representing PR diff chains.
43	
44	    Raises:
45	        NotImplementedError: Always — run on L4 VM with GITHUB_TOKEN.
46	    """
47	    raise NotImplementedError("Run on L4 VM with GITHUB_TOKEN")
48	
49	
50	def mine_issue_commit_chains(
51	    repo: str,
52	    max_issues: int = 100,
53	    github_token: str | None = None,
54	) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
55	    """Link GitHub issues to their fixing commits via commit message references.
56	
57	    Scans commit messages for "fixes #N", "closes #N", or "resolves #N"
58	    patterns to identify which commits address which issues. Groups linked
59	    commits as trajectory steps for distillation.
60	
61	    Returns trajectory dicts with the following fields:
62	    - task_id: f"issue_{repo}_{issue_number}" (e.g., "issue_owner/repo_123")
63	    - task_description: full issue body text
64	    - steps: list of commits referencing this issue in chronological order
65	    - outcome: "closed" when the issue was resolved, "open" if still open
66	
67	    Args:
68	        repo: GitHub repository in "owner/repo" format (e.g., "python/cpython").
69	        max_issues: Maximum number of issues to process. Defaults to 100.
70	        github_token: Personal access token for GitHub API authentication.
71	            Required for private repos and to avoid rate limiting.
72	
73	    Returns:
74	        List of trajectory dicts representing issue-commit chains.
75	
76	    Raises:
77	        NotImplementedError: Always — run on L4 VM with GITHUB_TOKEN.
78	    """
79	    raise NotImplementedError("Run on L4 VM with GITHUB_TOKEN")
80

1	"""Data preparation pipeline for context distillation training.
2	
3	Converts raw trajectory JSON files into a training JSONL by calling
4	format_for_distillation on each trajectory and persisting the resulting
5	records via save_jsonl.
6	
7	Usage (CLI):
8	    uv run python -m model_training.d2l_prep traj1.json traj2.json -o train.jsonl
9	"""
10	
11	from __future__ import annotations
12	
13	import json
14	import logging
15	from pathlib import Path
16	from typing import Any
17	
18	from model_training.d2l_data import format_for_distillation, save_jsonl
19	
20	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
21	
22	__all__ = ["prepare_training_jsonl"]
23	
24	
25	def _load_trajectories(path: Path) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
26	    """Load one or more trajectory dicts from a JSON file.
27	
28	    Handles both a single trajectory dict and a JSON array of trajectories.
29	
30	    Args:
31	        path: Path to a JSON file containing one trajectory dict or a list.
32	
33	    Returns:
34	        List of trajectory dicts (length >= 0).
35	    """
36	    raw: Any = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
37	    if isinstance(raw, list):
38	        return raw  # type: ignore[return-value]
39	    if isinstance(raw, dict):
40	        return [raw]
41	    logger.warning(
42	        "Unexpected JSON type in %s: %s — skipping", path, type(raw).__name__
43	    )
44	    return []
45	
46	
47	def prepare_training_jsonl(
48	    input_paths: list[Path],
49	    output_path: Path,
50	) -> int:
51	    """Convert trajectory JSON files to a training JSONL.
52	
53	    Reads each input file, calls format_for_distillation on every trajectory,
54	    collects all returned records, and writes them to output_path via save_jsonl.
55	
56	    Failed trajectories (outcome != 'success') are filtered by
57	    format_for_distillation and produce zero records — they do not raise.
58	
59	    Args:
60	        input_paths: Trajectory JSON files, each containing a single trajectory
61	            dict or a JSON array of trajectory dicts.
62	        output_path: Destination JSONL file. Parent directories are created
63	            automatically. File is always written (may be empty).
64	
65	    Returns:
66	        Number of records written.
67	    """
68	    all_records: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
69	
70	    for path in input_paths:
71	        trajectories = _load_trajectories(path)
72	        logger.info("Processing %s (%d trajectories)", path, len(trajectories))
73	        for traj in trajectories:
74	            records = format_for_distillation(traj)
75	            all_records.extend(records)
76	            logger.debug(
77	                "  trajectory %s → %d records",
78	                traj.get("task_id", "<no-id>"),
79	                len(records),
80	            )
81	
82	    save_jsonl(all_records, output_path)
83	    logger.info("Wrote %d records to %s", len(all_records), output_path)
84	    return len(all_records)
85	
86	
87	if __name__ == "__main__":
88	    import argparse
89	
90	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
91	        prog="python -m model_training.d2l_prep",
92	        description=(
93	            "Convert raw trajectory JSON files to a training JSONL "
94	            "for context distillation."
95	        ),
96	    )
97	    parser.add_argument(
98	        "input_paths",
99	        nargs="+",
100	        type=Path,
101	        help="One or more trajectory JSON files to process.",
102	    )
103	    parser.add_argument(
104	        "--output",
105	        "-o",
106	        required=True,
107	        type=Path,
108	        dest="output_path",
109	        help="Output JSONL file path.",
110	    )
111	
112	    args = parser.parse_args()
113	    n = prepare_training_jsonl(
114	        input_paths=args.input_paths,
115	        output_path=args.output_path,
116	    )
117	    print(f"Wrote {n} records to {args.output_path}")
118	
```

1	"""Tests for the d2l_prep data preparation pipeline.
2	
3	Covers prepare_training_jsonl (filters failed trajectories, writes JSONL)
4	and the CLI __main__ entry point.
5	"""
6	
7	from __future__ import annotations
8	
9	import json
10	import os
11	import subprocess
12	import sys
13	from pathlib import Path
14	
15	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
16	# Fixtures
17	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
18	
19	SUCCESSFUL_TRAJECTORY: dict = {
20	    "task_id": "task-001",
21	    "task_description": "Write a function that returns 42.",
22	    "steps": [
23	        {
24	            "description": "Wrote the function body.",
25	            "tests_passed": True,
26	            "canonical_solution": "def answer():\n    return 42",
27	        }
28	    ],
29	    "outcome": "success",
30	}
31	
32	FAILED_TRAJECTORY: dict = {
33	    "task_id": "task-002",
34	    "task_description": "Write a function that returns the string hello.",
35	    "steps": [
36	        {
37	            "description": "Attempted solution.",
38	            "tests_passed": False,
39	            "generated_code": "def answer():\n    return 'goodbye'",
40	        }
41	    ],
42	    "outcome": "failure",
43	}
44	
45	
46	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
47	# Test 1: successful trajectory produces records, failed trajectory is filtered
48	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
49	
50	
51	def test_prepare_training_jsonl_filters_failures(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
52	    """Records from failed trajectories are excluded; successful ones are included."""
53	    from model_training.d2l_prep import prepare_training_jsonl
54	
55	    success_file = tmp_path / "success.json"
56	    failure_file = tmp_path / "failure.json"
57	    output_file = tmp_path / "output.jsonl"
58	
59	    success_file.write_text(json.dumps(SUCCESSFUL_TRAJECTORY))
60	    failure_file.write_text(json.dumps(FAILED_TRAJECTORY))
61	
62	    count = prepare_training_jsonl(
63	        input_paths=[success_file, failure_file],
64	        output_path=output_file,
65	    )
66	
67	    assert output_file.exists(), "output file must be created"
68	    lines = [line for line in output_file.read_text().splitlines() if line.strip()]
69	    assert count == len(lines), "returned count must match line count"
70	    assert count > 0, "successful trajectory must produce at least one record"
71	
72	    for line in lines:
73	        record = json.loads(line)
74	        assert "activation_text" in record
75	        assert "teacher_text" in record
76	        assert "task_id" in record
77	
78	
79	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
80	# Test 2: no successful trajectories → empty file, count 0
81	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
82	
83	
84	def test_prepare_training_jsonl_all_failures_empty_output(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
85	    """When all trajectories fail, output JSONL has zero records."""
86	    from model_training.d2l_prep import prepare_training_jsonl
87	
88	    failure_file = tmp_path / "failure.json"
89	    output_file = tmp_path / "output.jsonl"
90	
91	    failure_file.write_text(json.dumps(FAILED_TRAJECTORY))
92	
93	    count = prepare_training_jsonl(
94	        input_paths=[failure_file],
95	        output_path=output_file,
96	    )
97	
98	    assert count == 0, "zero records expected for all-failure input"
99	    assert output_file.exists(), "output file must be created even when empty"
100	    lines = [line for line in output_file.read_text().splitlines() if line.strip()]
101	    assert len(lines) == 0
102	
103	
104	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
105	# Test 3: CLI --help exits 0
106	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
107	
108	
109	def test_cli_help_exits_zero() -> None:
110	    """Running python -m model_training.d2l_prep --help exits with code 0."""
111	    lib_root = Path(__file__).parent.parent
112	    env = {**os.environ, "PYTHONPATH": str(lib_root / "src")}
113	    result = subprocess.run(
114	        [sys.executable, "-m", "model_training.d2l_prep", "--help"],
115	        capture_output=True,
116	        text=True,
117	        cwd=lib_root,
118	        env=env,
119	    )
120	    assert result.returncode == 0, (
121	        f"--help exited {result.returncode}\n"
122	        f"stdout: {result.stdout}\nstderr: {result.stderr}"
123	    )
124	    assert "usage" in result.stdout.lower() or "usage" in result.stderr.lower(), (
125	        "help output must contain 'usage'"
126	    )
127

1	"""Tests for model_training.d2l_data module (TEST-02 + trajectory/augmentation).
2	
3	Tests cover 5 original data pipeline behaviors plus trajectory generation
4	and augmentation:
5	1. format_for_distillation produces records with activation_text and teacher_text fields
6	2. format_for_distillation activation_text has no answer, teacher_text has answer
7	3. generate_needle_dataset returns records with activation_text, teacher_text, task_id
8	4. save_jsonl / load_jsonl round-trip returns identical records
9	5. split_by_task_id produces train and test sets with zero task_id overlap
10	6. generate_trajectory_dataset returns records with required fields
11	7. generate_trajectory_dataset task_id starts with "HumanEval/"
12	8. augment_trajectories returns n_variants records per input
13	9. augmented records inherit source task_id
14	10. augmented + original records pass split_by_task_id with zero task-ID leakage
15	"""
16	
17	from __future__ import annotations
18	
19	import sys
20	from pathlib import Path
21	from types import ModuleType
22	from typing import Any
23	from unittest.mock import AsyncMock, MagicMock
24	
25	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
26	# Helpers
27	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
28	
29	
30	def _make_trajectory(
31	    task_id: str = "HumanEval/0",
32	    task_description: str = "Write a function that adds two numbers.",
33	    outcome: str = "success",
34	    answer: str = "def add(a, b):\n    return a + b",
35	) -> dict[str, Any]:
36	    """Create a minimal trajectory dict for format_for_distillation tests."""
37	    return {
38	        "task_id": task_id,
39	        "session_id": task_id,
40	        "task_description": task_description,
41	        "outcome": outcome,
42	        "steps": [
43	            {
44	                "attempt": 0,
45	                "generated_code": "def add(a, b): pass",
46	                "tests_passed": False,
47	                "description": "Initial attempt",
48	            },
49	            {
50	                "attempt": 1,
51	                "generated_code": answer,
52	                "tests_passed": True,
53	                "description": "Corrected implementation",
54	                "canonical_solution": answer,
55	            },
56	        ],
57	    }
58	
59	
60	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
61	# Test 1: format_for_distillation produces records with required fields
62	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
63	
64	
65	def test_format_for_distillation_produces_records_with_required_fields() -> None:
66	    """format_for_distillation returns records with activation_text and teacher_text."""
67	    from model_training.d2l_data import format_for_distillation
68	
69	    trajectory = _make_trajectory()
70	    records = format_for_distillation(trajectory)
71	
72	    assert len(records) > 0, "Expected at least one record from a successful trajectory"
73	    for record in records:
74	        assert "activation_text" in record, "Record missing 'activation_text' field"
75	        assert "teacher_text" in record, "Record missing 'teacher_text' field"
76	        assert "task_id" in record, "Record missing 'task_id' field"
77	
78	
79	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
80	# Test 2: activation_text has no answer, teacher_text has answer
81	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
82	
83	
84	def test_format_for_distillation_activation_has_no_answer_teacher_has_answer() -> None:
85	    """activation_text does NOT contain answer; teacher_text DOES contain answer."""
86	    from model_training.d2l_data import format_for_distillation
87	
88	    answer = "def add(a: int, b: int) -> int:\n    return a + b"
89	    trajectory = _make_trajectory(answer=answer)
90	    records = format_for_distillation(trajectory)
91	
92	    assert len(records) > 0
93	    record = records[0]
94	
95	    # activation_text must NOT contain the answer code
96	    assert answer not in record["activation_text"], (
97	        "activation_text must not contain the answer"
98	    )
99	    # teacher_text MUST contain the answer code
100	    assert answer in record["teacher_text"], "teacher_text must contain the answer"
101	
102	
103	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
104	# Test 3: generate_needle_dataset returns records with required fields
105	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
106	
107	
108	def test_generate_needle_dataset_returns_records_with_required_fields() -> None:
109	    """generate_needle_dataset returns list of dicts with required fields."""
110	    from model_training.d2l_data import generate_needle_dataset
111	
112	    records = generate_needle_dataset(n=5)
113	
114	    assert isinstance(records, list), "Expected a list of records"
115	    assert len(records) == 5, f"Expected 5 records, got {len(records)}"
116	    for i, record in enumerate(records):
117	        assert "activation_text" in record, f"Record {i} missing 'activation_text'"
118	        assert "teacher_text" in record, f"Record {i} missing 'teacher_text'"
119	        assert "task_id" in record, f"Record {i} missing 'task_id'"
120	
121	
122	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
123	# Test 4: save_jsonl / load_jsonl round-trip
124	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
125	
126	
127	def test_save_jsonl_load_jsonl_round_trip(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
128	    """save_jsonl then load_jsonl returns identical records."""
129	    from model_training.d2l_data import load_jsonl, save_jsonl
130	
131	    records: list[dict[str, Any]] = [
132	        {"task_id": "HumanEval/0", "activation_text": "A", "teacher_text": "A+ans"},
133	        {"task_id": "HumanEval/1", "activation_text": "B", "teacher_text": "B+ans"},
134	    ]
135	
136	    out_path = tmp_path / "test_data.jsonl"
137	    save_jsonl(records, out_path)
138	
139	    loaded = load_jsonl(out_path)
140	    assert loaded == records, "Round-trip JSONL load did not return identical records"

148	def test_split_by_task_id_zero_task_id_overlap() -> None:
149	    """split_by_task_id produces train and test with no task_id overlap."""
150	    from model_training.d2l_data import split_by_task_id
151	
152	    # Create records with 10 distinct task_ids (2 records each for variety)
153	    records: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
154	    for i in range(10):
155	        tid = f"HumanEval/{i}"
156	        records.extend(

162

166	    test_ids = {r["task_id"] for r in test}
167	
168	    overlap = train_ids & test_ids
169	    assert overlap == set(), f"Expected zero task_id overlap, got: {overlap}"
170	    assert len(test) > 0, "Test set should not be empty"
171	    assert len(train) > 0, "Train set should not be empty"
172	    assert len(train) + len(test) == len(records), (
173	        "All records should be in train or test"
174	    )

242	
243	def _inject_fake_datasets_module(fake_data: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> None:
244	    """Inject a fake datasets module into sys.modules for patching.
245	
246	    Avoids network calls to Hugging Face Hub in CI.
247	    """
248	    if "datasets" not in sys.modules:
249	        fake_datasets = ModuleType("datasets")
250	        sys.modules["datasets"] = fake_datasets
251	
252	    load_mock = MagicMock(return_value=fake_data)
253	    sys.modules["datasets"].load_dataset = load_mock  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
254	
255	
256	def _inject_fake_inference_modules(mock_provider_instance: MagicMock) -> None:
257	    """Inject fake inference.ollama_provider into sys.modules for patching.
258	
259	    Avoids importing the real inference library (which may require GPU deps).
260	    The fake module exposes a MockOllamaProvider class that returns the
261	    provided mock instance when constructed.
262	    """
263	    if "inference" not in sys.modules:
264	        fake_inference = ModuleType("inference")
265	        sys.modules["inference"] = fake_inference
266	
267	    if "inference.ollama_provider" not in sys.modules:
268	        fake_ollama_mod = ModuleType("inference.ollama_provider")
269	        sys.modules["inference.ollama_provider"] = fake_ollama_mod
270	
271	    # Make OllamaProvider(base_url=...) return the mock instance
272	    mock_class = MagicMock(return_value=mock_provider_instance)
273	    sys.modules["inference.ollama_provider"].OllamaProvider = mock_class  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
274	
275	
276	def _make_mock_provider() -> MagicMock:
277	    """Create a mock OllamaProvider whose generate method returns .text."""
278	    mock_result = MagicMock()
279	    mock_result.text = "augmented text content"
280	    provider = MagicMock()
281	    provider.generate = AsyncMock(return_value=mock_result)
282	    return provider
283

247	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
248	# Public API
249	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
250	
251	
252	def save_jsonl(records: list[dict[str, Any]], path: str | Path) -> None:
253	    """Persist a list of dicts to a JSONL file.
254	
255	    Args:
256	        records: List of JSON-serializable dicts to save.
257	        path: File path for output. Parent directories are created if needed.
258	    """
259	    out = Path(path)
260	    out.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
261	    with out.open("w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
262	        for record in records:
263	            f.write(json.dumps(record, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
264	
265	
266	def load_jsonl(path: str | Path) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
267	    """Load records from a JSONL file.
268	
269	    Args:
270	        path: Path to the JSONL file.
271	
272	    Returns:
273	        List of dicts, one per non-empty line.
274	    """
275	    src = Path(path)
276	    return [
277	        json.loads(line)
278	        for line in src.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines()
279	        if line.strip()
280	    ]
281	
282	
283	def format_for_distillation(trajectory: dict[str, Any]) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
284	    """Convert a trajectory to distillation records with activation/teacher split.
285	
286	    Each record has:
287	    - activation_text: trajectory context + task description (NO answer tokens)
288	    - teacher_text: trajectory context + task description + answer
289	    - task_id: identifier for train/test splitting
290	
291	    Only successful trajectories (outcome == 'success') produce records.
292	
293	    Args:
294	        trajectory: Trajectory dict with task_id/session_id, task_description,
295	            steps, and outcome fields.
296	
297	    Returns:
298	        List of distillation record dicts, or empty list if no successful outcome.
299	    """
300	    if trajectory.get("outcome") != "success":
301	        return []
302	
303	    steps: list[dict[str, Any]] = trajectory.get("steps", [])
304	    task_description: str = trajectory.get("task_description", "")
305	    task_id: str = trajectory.get("task_id") or trajectory.get("session_id", "")
306	
307	    # Build trajectory context from step descriptions (no answer tokens)
308	    trajectory_parts: list[str] = []
309	    for step in steps:
310	        desc = step.get("description", "")
311	        if desc:
312	            trajectory_parts.append(desc)
313	
314	    trajectory_text = "\n".join(trajectory_parts)
315	
316	    # Build activation_text (context only, no answer)
317	    activation_base = f"{trajectory_text}\n{task_description}".strip()
318	
319	    records: list[dict[str, str]] = []
320	    for step in steps:
321	        if not step.get("tests_passed"):
322	            continue
323	
324	        # Extract answer from canonical_solution or generated_code
325	        answer: str = step.get("canonical_solution") or step.get("generated_code", "")
326	        if not answer:
327	            continue
328	
329	        records.append(
330	            {
331	                "task_id": task_id,
332	                "activation_text": activation_base,
333	                "teacher_text": f"{activation_base}\n{answer}",
334	            }
335	        )
336	
337	    return records
338	
339	
340	def generate_needle_dataset(n: int = 20) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
341	    """Generate needle-in-haystack records for CI smoke testing.
342	
343	    Records are deterministic (no randomness, no LLM). Each record contains
344	    a code fact embedded in a function/class context with a query and answer.
345	
346	    Args:
347	        n: Number of records to generate. Cycles through templates if n exceeds
348	           the number of available templates.
349	
350	    Returns:
351	        List of n record dicts with activation_text, teacher_text, and task_id.
352	    """
353	    records: list[dict[str, str]] = []
354	    n_templates = len(_NEEDLE_TEMPLATES)
355	    n_slots = len(_SLOT_VALUES)
356	
357	    for i in range(n):
358	        template = _NEEDLE_TEMPLATES[i % n_templates]
359	        slots = _SLOT_VALUES[i % n_slots]
360	
361	        try:
362	            trajectory = template["trajectory_template"].format(**slots)
363	            query = template["query_template"].format(**slots)
364	            answer = template["answer_template"].format(**slots)
365	        except KeyError:
366	            # If template has a slot not in _SLOT_VALUES, use a safe fallback
367	            trajectory = (
368	                f"# code fact {i}\ndef func_{i}(x: int) -> int:\n    return {i}"
369	            )
370	            query = f"What does func_{i} return?"
371	            answer = str(i)
372	
373	        activation_text = f"{trajectory}\n\nQ: {query}"
374	        teacher_text = f"{trajectory}\n\nQ: {query}\nA: {answer}"
375	
376	        records.append(
377	            {
378	                "task_id": f"needle_{i}",
379	                "activation_text": activation_text,
380	                "teacher_text": teacher_text,
381	            }
382	        )
383	
384	    return records
385	
386	
387	def generate_trajectory_dataset(
388	    source: str = "humaneval",
389	    max_tasks: int | None = None,
390	) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
391	    """Generate trajectory records from a coding task dataset.
392	
393	    Each record has activation_text (prompt only, no solution) and teacher_text
394	    (prompt + canonical solution), making it ready for KL-divergence distillation.
395	
396	    Args:
397	        source: Dataset source identifier. Currently only "humaneval" is supported.
398	        max_tasks: Maximum number of tasks to process. If None, all tasks are used.
399	
400	    Returns:
401	        List of trajectory record dicts with task_id, activation_text, and
402	        teacher_text fields.
403	
404	    Raises:
405	        ValueError: If an unsupported source is specified.
406	    """
407	    from datasets import load_dataset  # noqa: PLC0415
408	
409	    if source == "humaneval":
410	        dataset = load_dataset("openai_humaneval", split="test", trust_remote_code=True)
411	    else:
412	        raise ValueError(
413	            f"Unsupported source: {source!r}. Only 'humaneval' is supported."
414	        )
415	
416	    records: list[dict[str, str]] = []
417	    tasks = list(dataset) if max_tasks is None else list(dataset)[:max_tasks]
418	    for task in tasks:
419	        task_id: str = task["task_id"]
420	        prompt: str = task["prompt"]
421	        canonical_solution: str = task["canonical_solution"]
422	
423	        # activation_text: the function signature + docstring (no solution)
424	        activation_text = prompt.rstrip()
425	        # teacher_text: prompt + solution (the full implementation)
426	        teacher_text = prompt.rstrip() + canonical_solution
427	
428	        records.append(
429	            {
430	                "task_id": task_id,
431	                "activation_text": activation_text,
432	                "teacher_text": teacher_text,
433	            }
434	        )
435	
436	    logger.info("Generated %d trajectory records from %r", len(records), source)
437	    return records
438	
439	
440	def augment_trajectories(
441	    trajectories: list[dict[str, Any]],
442	    n_variants: int = 3,
443	    model: str = "qwen2.5-coder:1.5b",
444	    ollama_base_url: str | None = None,
445	) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
446	    """Produce LLM-augmented variants of trajectory records.
447	
448	    For each input trajectory, generates up to n_variants augmented records
449	    using an Ollama LLM. Augmented records always inherit the source task_id
450	    to preserve split integrity when mixed with originals.
451	
452	    Augmentation strategies (up to n_variants selected in order):
453	    1. Paraphrase the task description
454	    2. Reorder/drop steps in the trajectory
455	    3. Rename variables throughout the trajectory
456	
457	    Args:
458	        trajectories: List of trajectory record dicts with task_id,
459	            activation_text, and teacher_text fields.
460	        n_variants: Number of augmented variants to produce per trajectory.
461	            Maximum 3 (one per augmentation strategy).
462	        model: Ollama model identifier to use for augmentation.
463	        ollama_base_url: Ollama base URL. Defaults to "http://localhost:11434".
464	
465	    Returns:
466	        List of augmented trajectory dicts. Each record has the same task_id
467	        as its source trajectory, with LLM-generated activation_text and
468	        teacher_text.
469	    """
470	    import asyncio  # noqa: PLC0415
471	
472	    from inference.ollama_provider import OllamaProvider  # noqa: PLC0415
473	
474	    base_url = ollama_base_url or "http://localhost:11434"
475	    provider = OllamaProvider(base_url=base_url)
476	
477	    augmentation_prompts = [
478	        (
479	            "Paraphrase the following coding task description and trajectory, "
480	            "keeping the same logic but using different wording:\n\n{text}"
481	        ),
482	        (
483	            "Rewrite the following coding trajectory by reordering and slightly "
484	            "dropping some intermediate steps, while preserving the overall "
485	            "outcome:\n\n{text}"
486	        ),
487	        (
488	            "Rewrite the following code trajectory by renaming variables and "
489	            "function parameters to different names, keeping the same logic:"
490	            "\n\n{text}"
491	        ),
492	    ]
493	
494	    async def _augment_one(
495	        trajectory: dict[str, Any],
496	        prov: Any,
497	        n: int,
498	        mdl: str,
499	    ) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
500	        task_id: str = trajectory["task_id"]
501	        activation_text: str = trajectory.get("activation_text", "")
502	        teacher_text: str = trajectory.get("teacher_text", "")
503	
504	        results: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
505	        strategies = augmentation_prompts[:n]
506	        for strategy_prompt in strategies:
507	            augment_prompt = strategy_prompt.format(text=teacher_text)
508	            result = await prov.generate(augment_prompt, mdl)
509	            augmented_teacher = result.text
510	
511	            # Build augmented activation by stripping the solution portion
512	            # Use teacher text length ratio to approximate activation
513	            activation_prompt = strategy_prompt.format(text=activation_text)
514	            act_result = await prov.generate(activation_prompt, mdl)
515	            augmented_activation = act_result.text
516	
517	            results.append(
518	                {
519	                    "task_id": task_id,  # CRITICAL: inherit source task_id
520	                    "activation_text": augmented_activation,
521	                    "teacher_text": augmented_teacher,
522	                }
523	            )
524	        return results
525	
526	    async def _augment_all(
527	        trajs: list[dict[str, Any]],
528	        n: int,
529	        mdl: str,
530	    ) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
531	        all_tasks = [_augment_one(t, provider, n, mdl) for t in trajs]
532	        nested = await asyncio.gather(*all_tasks)
533	        return [record for group in nested for record in group]
534	
535	    augmented = asyncio.run(_augment_all(trajectories, n_variants, model))
536	    logger.info(
537	        "Augmented %d trajectories into %d records (n_variants=%d)",
538	        len(trajectories),
539	        len(augmented),
540	        n_variants,
541	    )
542	    return augmented
543	
544	
545	def split_by_task_id(
546	    records: list[dict[str, Any]],
547	    test_fraction: float = 0.2,
548	    seed: int = 42,
549	) -> tuple[list[dict[str, Any]], list[dict[str, Any]]]:
550	    """Split records at task-ID boundary with no task_id crossing train/test.
551	
552	    Augmented records that share a task_id are always assigned to the same
553	    partition, preventing task-family leakage.
554	
555	    Args:
556	        records: List of record dicts, each with a 'task_id' field.
557	        test_fraction: Fraction of unique task_ids to assign to test set.
558	            Minimum 1 task_id goes to test even if fraction rounds to 0.
559	        seed: Random seed for reproducible splits.
560	
561	    Returns:
562	        Tuple of (train_records, test_records) where task_ids never overlap.
563	    """
564	    task_ids = sorted({r["task_id"] for r in records})
565	    rng = random.Random(seed)
566	    rng.shuffle(task_ids)
567	    n_test = max(1, int(len(task_ids) * test_fraction))
568	    test_ids = set(task_ids[:n_test])
569	    train = [r for r in records if r["task_id"] not in test_ids]
570	    test = [r for r in records if r["task_id"] in test_ids]
571	    return train, test
572	
```

\"https://api.github.com\"\n_LINK_NEXT_RE = re.compile(r'<([^>]+)>;\\s*rel=\"next\"')\n\n\nclass GitHubClient:\n    \"\"\"Minimal GitHub REST API client with pagination and rate-limit handling.\n\n    Args:\n        token: GitHub personal access token. base_url.rstrip(\"/\")\n        self._headers: dict[str, str] = {\n            \"Accept\": \"application/vnd.github+json\",\n            \"X-GitHub-Api-Version\": \"2022-11-28\",\n        }\n        if token:\n            self._headers[\"Authorization\"] = f\"Bearer {token}\"\n\n    def get(\n        self,\n        path: str,\n        params: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n        max_retries: int = 3,\n    ) -> Any:\n        \"\"\"GET a single API endpoint, with rate-limit retry.\n\n        Args:\n            path: API path (e.g., \"/repos/owner/repo/pulls\").\n            params: Query parameters.\n            max_retries: Number of retries on rate-limit 403.\n\n        Returns:\n            Parsed JSON response.\n        \"\"\"\n        url = f\"{self._base_url}{path}\" if path.startswith(\"/\") else path\n        for attempt in range(max_retries + 1):\n            resp = httpx.get(url, headers=self._headers, params=params, timeout=30.0)\n            if resp.status_code == 403 and \"rate limit\" in resp.json().get(\"message\", \"\").lower():\n                wait = int(resp.headers.get(\"Retry-After\", \"60\"))\n                logger.warning(\"Rate limited, waiting %ds (attempt %d/%d)\", wait, attempt + 1, max_retries)\n                time.sleep(wait)\n                continue\n            resp.raise_for_status()\n            return resp.json()\n        resp.raise_for_status()\n        return resp.json()  # unreachable, satisfies mypy\n\n    def get_paginated(\n        self,\n        path: str,\n        params: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n        max_pages: int = 10,\n        per_page: int = 100,\n    ) -> list[Any]:\n        \"\"\"GET a paginated list endpoint, following Link: next headers.\n\n        Args:\n            path: API path returning a JSON array.\n            params: Extra query parameters.\n            max_pages: Stop after this many pages.\n            per_page: Items per page (max 100 for most endpoints).\n\n        Returns:\n            Flat list of all items across pages.\n        \"\"\"\n        all_items: list[Any] = []\n        merged_params = {**(params or {}), \"per_page\": per_page}\n        url: str | None = f\"{self._base_url}{path}\" if path.startswith(\"/\") else path\n\n        for _ in range(max_pages):\n            if url is None:\n                break\n            resp = httpx.get(url, headers=self._headers, params=merged_params, timeout=30.0)\n            resp.raise_for_status()\n            items = resp.json()\n            all_items.extend(items)\n\n            # Parse Link header for next page\n            link_header = resp.headers.get(\"Link\", \"\")\n            match = _LINK_NEXT_RE.search(link_header)\n            url = match.group(1) if match else None\n            # After the first request, params are encoded in the next URL\n            merged_params = {}\n\n        return all_items\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Run tests to verify they pass**\n\nRun: `cd The full file becomes:\n\n```python\n\"\"\"GitHub trajectory mining for coding session distillation.\n\nDesigned to run on an L4 VM with network access, not in CI.\nRequires a GitHub token for authentication and rate-limit avoidance.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport logging\nimport math\nimport re\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom model_training.github_client import GitHubClient\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n__all__ = [\"mine_pr_diff_chains\", \"mine_issue_commit_chains\"]\n\n_FIXES_RE = re.compile(\n    r\"(?:fix(?:es)?|close[sd]?|resolve[sd]?)\\s+#(\\d+)\",\n    re.IGNORECASE,\n)\n\n\ndef mine_pr_diff_chains(\n    repo: str,\n    max_prs: int = 100,\n    github_token: str | None = None,\n) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n    \"\"\"Extract PR diff chains from a GitHub repository.\n\n    Each chain represents an iterative coding session: initial commit ->\n    review comments -> revision commits. The resulting trajectory records\n    capture the back-and-forth of code review as a multi-step improvement\n    process, suitable for distillation.\n\n    Returns trajectory dicts with the following fields:\n    - task_id: f\"pr_{repo}_{pr_number}\" (e.g., \"pr_owner/repo_42\")\n    - task_description: PR title concatenated with body text\n    - steps: list of step dicts with type (\"commit\"/\"review\"), content, and description\n    - outcome: \"merged\" or \"closed\" depending on PR final state\n\n    Args:\n        repo: GitHub repository in \"owner/repo\" format (e.g., \"python/cpython\").\n        max_prs: Maximum number of PRs to process. Defaults to 100.\n        github_token: Personal access token for GitHub API authentication.\n            Required for private repos and to avoid rate limiting.\n\n    Returns:\n        List of trajectory dicts representing PR diff chains.\n    \"\"\"\n    client = GitHubClient(token=github_token)\n    max_pages = max(1, math.ceil(max_prs / 100))\n\n    prs: list[dict[str, Any]] = client.get_paginated(\n        f\"/repos/{repo}/pulls\",\n        params={\"state\": \"closed\", \"sort\": \"updated\", \"direction\": \"desc\"},\n        max_pages=max_pages,\n    )\n    prs = prs[:max_prs]\n\n    if not prs:\n        return []\n\n    trajectories: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n\n    for pr in prs:\n        pr_number: int = pr[\"number\"]\n        title: str = pr.get(\"title\", \"\")\n        body: str = pr.get(\"body\", \"\") or \"\"\n        merged: bool = pr.get(\"merged_at\") is not None\n\n        # Fetch commits for this PR\n        commits: list[dict[str, Any]] = client.get_paginated(\n            f\"/repos/{repo}/pulls/{pr_number}/commits\",\n            max_pages=5,\n        )\n\n        # Fetch review comments for this PR\n        review_comments: list[dict[str, Any]] = client.get_paginated(\n            f\"/repos/{repo}/pulls/{pr_number}/comments\",\n            max_pages=3,\n        )\n\n        # Build steps: commit diffs + review comments\n        steps: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n\n        for commit in commits:\n            sha: str = commit[\"sha\"]\n            msg: str = commit[\"commit\"][\"message\"]\n            # Fetch full commit detail (includes file patches)\n            detail: dict[str, Any] = client.get(f\"/repos/{repo}/commits/{sha}\")\n            patches: list[str] = []\n            for file_info in detail.get(\"files\", []):\n                patch = file_info.get(\"patch\", \"\")\n                if patch:\n                    patches.append(f\"--- {file_info['filename']} ---\\n{patch}\")\n            steps.append({\n                \"type\": \"commit\",\n                \"description\": msg,\n                \"content\": \"\\n\".join(patches),\n            })\n\n        for comment in review_comments:\n            steps.append({\n                \"type\": \"review\",\n                \"description\": \"Review comment\",\n                \"content\": comment.get(\"body\", \"\"),\n            })\n\n        trajectories.append({\n            \"task_id\": f\"pr_{repo}_{pr_number}\",\n            \"task_description\": f\"{title}\\n\\n{body}\".strip(),\n            \"steps\": steps,\n            \"outcome\": \"merged\" if merged else \"closed\",\n        })\n\n    logger.info(\"Mined %d PR diff chains from %s\", len(trajectories), repo)\n    return trajectories\n\n\ndef mine_issue_commit_chains(\n    repo: str,\n    max_issues: int = 100,\n    github_token: str | None = None,\n) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n    \"\"\"Link GitHub issues to their fixing commits via commit message references.\n\n    Scans commit messages for \"fixes #N\", \"closes #N\", or \"resolves #N\"\n    patterns to identify which commits address which issues. Groups linked\n    commits as trajectory steps for distillation.\n\n    Returns trajectory dicts with the following fields:\n    - task_id: f\"issue_{repo}_{issue_number}\" (e.g., \"issue_owner/repo_123\")\n    - task_description: full issue body text\n    - steps: list of commits referencing this issue in chronological order\n    - outcome: \"closed\" when the issue was resolved, \"open\" if still open\n\n    Args:\n        repo: GitHub repository in \"owner/repo\" format (e.g., \"python/cpython\").\n        max_issues: Maximum number of issues to process. Defaults to 100.\n        github_token: Personal access token for GitHub API authentication.\n            Required for private repos and to avoid rate limiting.\n\n    Returns:\n        List of trajectory dicts representing issue-commit chains.\n    \"\"\"\n    raise NotImplementedError(\"Run on L4 VM with GITHUB_TOKEN\")\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Run tests to verify they pass**\n\nRun: `cd /Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune && uv run pytest libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_mining.py::TestMinePrDiffChains -v`\nExpected: All 6 tests PASS.\n\n- [ ] **Step 5: Lint**\n\nRun: `cd \"fixes #N\", \"closes #N\", or \"resolves #N\"\n    patterns to identify which commits address which issues. Groups linked\n    commits as trajectory steps for distillation.\n\n    Returns trajectory dicts with the following fields:\n    - task_id: f\"issue_{repo}_{issue_number}\" (e.g., \"issue_owner/repo_123\")\n    - task_description: full issue body text\n    - steps: list of commits referencing this issue in chronological order\n    - outcome: \"closed\" when the issue was resolved, \"open\" if still open\n\n    Args:\n        repo: GitHub repository in \"owner/repo\" format (e.g., \"python/cpython\").\n        max_issues: Maximum number of issues to process. trajectory dicts representing issue-commit chains.\n    \"\"\"\n    client = GitHubClient(token=github_token)\n    max_pages = max(1, math.ceil(max_issues / 100))\n\n    # Fetch closed + open issues (GitHub /issues also returns PRs, filter them)\n    raw_issues: list[dict[str, Any]] = client.get_paginated(\n        f\"/repos/{repo}/issues\",\n        params={\"state\": \"all\", \"sort\": \"updated\", \"direction\": \"desc\"},\n        max_pages=max_pages,\n    )\n    # Filter out pull requests (they have a \"pull_request\" key)\n    issues = [i for i in raw_issues if not i.get(\"pull_request\")]\n    issues = issues[:max_issues]\n\n    if not issues:\n        return []\n\n    # Build a set of issue numbers we care about\n    issue_numbers = {i[\"number\"] for i in issues}\n    issue_map: dict[int, dict[str, Any]] = {i[\"number\"]: i for i in issues}\n\n    # Fetch recent commits and scan for \"fixes/closes/resolves #N\"\n    commits: list[dict[str, Any]] = client.get_paginated(\n        f\"/repos/{repo}/commits\",\n        max_pages=10,\n    )\n\n    # Map issue_number -> list of (sha, message) in chronological order\n    linked: dict[int, list[dict[str, Any]]] = {n: [] for n in issue_numbers}\n\n    for commit in commits:\n        msg: str = commit[\"commit\"][\"message\"]\n        matches = _FIXES_RE.findall(msg)\n        for issue_num_str in matches:\n            issue_num = int(issue_num_str)\n            if issue_num in linked:\n                linked[issue_num].append(commit)\n\n    # Build trajectories for issues that have linked commits\n    trajectories: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n\n    for issue_num, issue_commits in linked.items():\n        if not issue_commits:\n            continue\n\n        issue = issue_map[issue_num]\n        steps: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n\n        for commit in issue_commits:\n            sha = commit[\"sha\"]\n            detail: dict[str, Any] = client.get(f\"/repos/{repo}/commits/{sha}\")\n            patches: list[str] = []\n            for file_info in detail.get(\"files\", []):\n                patch = file_info.get(\"patch\", \"\")\n                if patch:\n                    patches.append(f\"--- {file_info['filename']} ---\\n{patch}\")\n            steps.append({\n                \"type\": \"commit\",\n                \"description\": commit[\"commit\"][\"message\"],\n                \"content\": \"\\n\".join(patches),\n            })\n\n        trajectories.append({\n            \"task_id\": f\"issue_{repo}_{issue_num}\",\n            \"task_description\": (issue.get(\"body\", \"\") or \"\").strip(),\n            \"steps\": steps,\n            \"outcome\": issue.get(\"state\", \"open\"),\n        })\n\n    logger.info(\"Mined %d issue-commit chains from %s\", len(trajectories), repo)\n    return trajectories\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Run tests to verify they pass**\n\nRun: normalized_step[\"canonical_solution\"] = step.get(\"content\", \"\")\n        normalized_steps.append(normalized_step)\n\n    return {\n        \"task_id\": mined.get(\"task_id\", \"\"),\n ## COMPREHENSIVE FINDINGS REPORT: Trajectories, Adapters, and Training in Rune

Based on my thorough exploration of the Rune repository documentation, I can now provide you with a complete picture of the training ecosystem, trajectory design, and adapter system architecture.

---

## 1. WHAT MAKES A GOOD TRAJECTORY FOR TRAINING

From **Background (docs/article/background.md)** and **Training_Review.md**, trajectories must capture three structural properties:

### **Temporal Ordering**
- Steps have meaningful **causal sequence**: generate → execute → error → diagnose → fix
- This temporal structure carries causal information absent from static documents or simple examples
- The sequence itself is the training signal, not just the endpoint

### **Procedural Abstraction**
- Trajectories encode HOW to solve problems (procedures), not WHAT is true (facts) or WHAT examples look like (demonstrations)
- They are **action-oriented**: operations, preconditions, effects
- Each step builds on prior failures and successes

### **Self-Correcting Structure**
- Trajectories naturally contain failure-recovery patterns: attempt → error → diagnosis → fix
- Both the **error pattern AND its resolution** are encoded in the same training signal
- This is absent from documents, instructions, and static examples
- Critical for Rune's diagnose→repair loop (Phase 5)

### **Programming by Backprop (PBB) Connection**
From **some_new_insights.md**: Cook et al. (2025) found that:
- **Code internalizes algorithmic abstractions via backpropagation** — LLMs can evaluate programs on new inputs without seeing those inputs in-context
- **Code > natural language** for abstraction learning — programs are significantly better training signals than semantic descriptions
- **Robustness across distributions** — code-trained models generalize better than I/O-trained models
- This validates trajectories as the right modality: they contain code, execution context, and error patterns

**Expected trajectory corpus**: 50–100 diverse trajectories spanning 3+ task types (bug-fix, feature-impl, refactor). Mix of successful and exhausted trajectories — exhausted ones encode critical failure patterns.

---

## 2. TRAJECTORY RENDERING: PROMPTS VS. ADAPTER CONTEXT

### **Skeleton Prompts** 
From the template files (`libs/shared/src/shared/templates/`):

The system uses **two-phase templating**:

1. **Prose Templates** (for LLM prompts to humans):
   - `prompt_code.j2` — Instructs the model to write code
   - `prompt_diagnose.j2` — Asks "Which subtasks need repair? Output a numbered list"
   - `prompt_plan.j2` — Architecture planning instruction
   - `prompt_decompose.j2` — Task decomposition

2. **Skeleton Templates** (for internal trajectory context):
   - `code.j2` — Renders subtask code: `{{ subtask.name }}, {{ subtask_index }}/{{ total_subtasks }}, DESCRIPTION, COMPLETED DEPENDENCIES, PLAN, EXISTING CODE`
   - `plan.j2` — Renders architecture plan
   - `diagnose.j2` — Integration diagnostician template with `PROJECT, SUBTASK CODE, INTEGRATION ERROR, PRIOR REPAIR ATTEMPTS`

### **Key Distinction: Prompts vs Adapter Context**

**In the Agent Loop (generate-execute-reflect)**:
- Prompt templates (skeleton) are rendered with **task context only**
- Trajectory text is **passed to hypernetwork for adapter generation** (activation extraction)
- Prompts are stripped of trajectory content; adapters encode it as weights

**For Hypernetwork Training (D2L)**:
- Trajectories are rendered using **skeleton templates** (not full prose prompts)
- The skeleton contains: task description, prior code, error messages, fixes
- This skeleton is tokenized and fed to the base model for **activation extraction** (teacher forward pass)
- The hypernetwork then generates LoRA weights that reconstruct the teacher's behavior

**The "Skeleton Prompts + Prose Trajectories" Finding**:
From **Report_2_LoRA_Fine_Tuning_Strategy.md** (Section 3.3):
- Skeleton prompts are lightweight context injections (300-600 chars)
- They encode: subtask name, current attempt number, completed dependencies
- Full trajectories (prose) are rendered separately for distillation
- This separation allows adapters to encode procedural knowledge without bloating the prompt

---

## 3. THE ADAPTER SCALING FINDING: 0.16x OPTIMAL

From **Report_2_LoRA_Fine_Tuning_Strategy.md** (Section 3.4, Table: Training Parameters):

```
Scaling factor: 0.16 (Bayesian-optimized over 200 trials)
Rationale: "Sakana default of 45.25× is far too aggressive"
```

### **What This Means**

The Sakana checkpoint uses a **lora_alpha scaling convention** that is extremely aggressive. When transferred to Rune's trajectory domain:

- **Sakana's default**: `scaling = 45.25×` (lora_alpha / rank)
- **Bayesian-optimized for trajectories**: `scaling = 0.16×`
- **278× difference** — nearly 3 orders of magnitude

**Why the drastic reduction?**
- Sakana's scaling was optimized for document QA (static factual retrieval)
- Trajectories contain procedural knowledge, not factual content
- Aggressive scaling causes **excessive weight updates** that override base model behavior
- For coding tasks, conservative scaling preserves base model reasoning while layering procedural patterns

### **Implementation (from sakana_d2l.py)**
```python
peft_alpha = checkpoint_alpha * actual_rank * scaling_factor
# where scaling_factor = 0.16 (Bayesian-optimized)
```

This compensates for PEFT's internal `alpha/r` scaling: setting `peft_alpha = original_alpha * r * 0.16` recovers properly-scaled adapters.

---

## 4. THE TWO-STEP DIAGNOSE→REPAIR PATTERN

From **code_repair.j2**, **diagnose.j2**, and the phased pipeline:

### **Phase 1: Diagnose**
```
Role: integration-diagnostician
Inputs: 
  - Project context (300 chars)
  - Subtask code outputs (600 chars each)
  - Integration error (500 chars)
  - Prior repair attempts (iteration count, diagnosed subtasks, result score)

Output: Numbered list
  1. subtask_name — what is wrong and how to fix it
  2. ...
```

**Key feature**: Template tracks `repair_history` — prior iterations are included as context to avoid repeating failed diagnoses.

### **Phase 2: Repair**
```
Role: python-coder (repair)
Inputs:
  - Diagnosis from phase 1
  - Current code (800 chars)
  - Sibling subtasks (skeleton code, 400 chars each)
  - Prior repair attempts

Output: Fixed code
  - All classes/functions defined locally
  - No imports from sibling modules (encapsulation)
  - Dataclasses, type annotations, unittest.TestCase tests
```

### **Why This Pattern Matters for Training**

The trajectory records this two-step sequence:
1. **Diagnose step** identifies failure modes, root causes
2. **Repair step** applies targeted fixes

Adapters trained on these trajectories learn to:
- **Recognize error patterns** (diagnosis acts as pattern encoder)
- **Apply targeted fixes** (repair acts as correction encoder)
- **Iterate on prior attempts** (repair history provides context)

This maps directly to PBB's finding that **code + chain-of-thought reasoning is more reliable than implicit evaluation**. The adapter encodes both the procedure and the explicit reasoning about it.

---

## 5. WHAT THE TRAINING DATA NEEDS TO CAPTURE

From **Training_Review.md** (Section: "Where the data comes from") and **d2l_data.py**:

### **Current Training Data Sources**

#### **Real Coding Trajectories** (Primary)
Each trajectory JSON contains:
```json
{
  "session_id": "rune-abc123",
  "task_description": "Fix import error in module X",
  "task_type": "bug-fix",
  "steps": [
    {
      "attempt_number": 1,
      "generated_code": "...",
      "stdout": "...",
      "stderr": "ModuleNotFoundError: ...",
      "exit_code": 1,
      "tests_passed": false
    },
    {
      "attempt_number": 2,
      "generated_code": "...",
      "stdout": "...",
      "stderr": "",
      "exit_code": 0,
      "tests_passed": true
    }
  ],
  "outcome": "success"  // or "exhausted"
}
```

**Critical fields**:
- `generated_code` — the actual code produced (not a description)
- `tests_passed` — binary ground truth for success
- `stdout`, `stderr` — execution feedback
- Complete sequence — not just the final solution, but the PROCESS

#### **Synthetic Distillation Records** (For D2L Training)
From **d2l_data.py**, `format_for_distillation()` requires:
```python
{
  "activation_text": "Task: [task description] Attempts: [trajectory parts]",
  "teacher_text": "[task description]\n[successful_step generated_code]"
}
```

### **Critical Data Gaps** (Training_Review.md, Section "Key gaps")

**Gap A: Session ID Mismatch**
- Swarm training requests use `session_id = task_hash`
- Rune agent generates `session_id = f"rune-{uuid[:8]}"`
- Result: Training can't find trajectory files → falls back to synthetic data

**Gap B: No Trajectory Recording in Standard Mode**
- Standard swarm execution doesn't call `record_trajectory()`
- Only Rune's phased pipeline records trajectories
- Most swarm data never feeds back into training

**Gap C: Trajectory Step Schema Mismatch**
- `d2l_prep.py` expects `step["description"]` and `step["canonical_solution"]`
- Rune agent only provides: `generated_code, stdout, stderr, exit_code, tests_passed`
- Missing rich procedural context → activation_text collapses to just task_description

**Gap D: PR/Issue Mining Stubbed**
- `d2l_mining.py` has functions to mine GitHub trajectories but they raise `NotImplementedError`
- Only local agent trajectories and synthetic data are used

### **What the Data Must Encode**

From **methods.md** (Training Data Strategy section) and **background.md**:

1. **Temporal sequencing**: Each step must be ordered (not interchangeable)
2. **Procedural abstraction**: Code representations, not just input-output pairs
3. **Error patterns**: Both failures AND fixes
4. **Execution context**: Stdout, stderr, exit codes (real feedback, not synthetic)
5. **Attempt metadata**: Which attempt number, when did tests pass
6. **Self-correction**: Prior failed attempts should be included, not hidden

**From PBB insight**: The model should learn to **internalize the procedure in weight space**, not just memorize successful outputs. This requires:
- Complete execution traces (what the code actually does)
- Error messages (what went wrong)
- Diagnostic information (why it failed)
- Corrected code (how it was fixed)

All in a single coherent trajectory, not scattered across separate examples.

---

## 6. HOW THE ADAPTER SYSTEM ENCODES DOMAIN KNOWLEDGE

### **The Architecture (Five-Service Design)**

From **methods.md**:

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                  Adapter Registry (SQLite + Files)          │
│            (Write-once, immutable, content-addressed)       │
└────────┬──────────────┬──────────────┬──────────────┬────────┘
         │              │              │              │
    ┌────▼────┐  ┌──────▼──────┐ ┌────▼────┐  ┌─────▼──────┐
    │ rune    │  │ training    │ │evolution│  │ lora       │
    │ agent   │  │ svc         │ │ svc     │  │ server     │
    │ (loop)  │  │ (D2L/QLoRA) │ │ (TIES)  │  │ (vLLM+     │
    └─────────┘  └─────────────┘ └─────────┘  │  S-LoRA)   │
                                               └────────────┘
```

### **Three-Level Hierarchy** (Memory Composition Strategy)

Adapters are organized at three granularities:

1. **Project Level**: Broad patterns for a codebase
   - Architectural conventions
   - API design patterns
   - Testing practices
   - Domain-specific idioms

2. **Domain Level**: Patterns spanning projects
   - API design optimization
   - Database query patterns
   - Algorithm implementations
   - Error handling strategies

3. **Task Level**: Fine-grained, specific knowledge
   - Particular patterns for particular problems
   - Task-type-specific fixes
   - Implementation-specific optimizations

### **Two Training Paths (Sequential, Not Parallel)**

From **Report_1_Qwen_Migration_Technical_Assessment.md**:

#### **Stage 1: QLoRA Bootstrapping**
- Input: Real coding trajectories
- Method: Supervised fine-tuning on trajectory SFT data
- Process:
  ```python
  trajectory_json → format_for_sft() → [sys, user, assistant] messages
                 → SFTTrainer with NF4 quantized base
                 → rank-64 LoRA adapter
  ```
- Output: 50–100 high-quality adapters
- Duration: Minutes per adapter (GPU-bound)
- Warm-start: DeltaCoder weights (Qwen3.5-specific self-correction adapter)

#### **Stage 2: Hypernetwork Production**
- Input: Stage 1 adapter corpus (50–100 rank-64 QLoRA adapters)
- Method: Doc-to-LoRA hypernetwork trained via KL-divergence distillation
- Process:
  ```python
  Trajectory text → Base model activation extraction → Perceiver encoder
                → Hypernetwork weight head → rank-8 LoRA weights
  ```
- Output: Trained hypernetwork (generates rank-8 adapters in milliseconds)
- Transfer: Sakana `qwen_4b_d2l` aggregator (freeze aggregator, retrain head)
- Latency: Milliseconds per adapter (CPU-capable)

### **Knowledge Encoding Mechanism**

**How trajectories become adapter weights**:

From **sakana_d2l.py** and **d2l_train.py**:

```
1. TRAJECTORY TEXT ENCODING
   trajectory_text (task description + code + errors) 
   → tokenize → input_ids (1D array of token indices)

2. ACTIVATION EXTRACTION  
   input_ids → base model forward pass
   → extract hidden states from selected layers [L_sel layers]
   → shape: (batch=1, seq_len, hidden_dim=4096)

3. PERCEIVER ENCODING
   features (hidden states) → cross-attention with learned latents
   → per-layer latent projections
   → fixed-size latent representation (32 latents × 256 dims = 8,192 dims)

4. WEIGHT HEAD PROJECTION
   latent_repr → linear(8192 → ~25M params)
   → produces flattened [B, A] weight matrices for all layers

5. RESHAPE TO PEFT STRUCTURE
   flattened weights → reshape to per-layer (B, A) pairs
   → per_layer_B: shape (hidden_dim, rank)
   → per_layer_A: shape (rank, hidden_dim)

6. ADAPTER ASSEMBLY
   (B, A) pairs → safetensors file + PEFT config
   → registered in SQLite with metadata (task_type, fitness_score, etc.)
```

### **What Gets Encoded**

The adapter's parameter deltas encode:

1. **Procedural patterns** — How to solve the task class (from trajectory code)
2. **Error recovery** — How to diagnose and fix failures (from error messages + fixes)
3. **Domain semantics** — Task-specific vocabulary and patterns (from task description)
4. **Execution knowledge** — What execution looks like for this task (from stdout/stderr)

All in **weight space**, not prompt space. The adapter modifies base model layers to apply these patterns without expanding the context window.

### **Why Adapters ≠ RAG**

From **background.md**:
- **RAG (token-space memory)**: Retrieves trajectory snippets, injects into context
  - Pro: Full trajectory fidelity, searchable
  - Con: Consumes scarce context tokens, scales poorly
- **Adapters (weight-space memory)**: Trajectory distilled into parameters
  - Pro: No context cost, composable, scalable (S-LoRA)
  - Con: Requires training, less transparent

Rune chooses **composable weight-space memory** because:
1. Preserves base model as stable foundation
2. Enables independent adapter loading/removal
3. Supports multi-adapter composition through Evolution Operator
4. Scales to hundreds of adapters per model instance

---

## 7. THE EVOLUTION OPERATOR: FITNESS-DRIVEN LIFECYCLE

From **methods.md** (Evolution Operator section):

### **Fitness Formula**
```
φ(a) = α · pass_rate(a) + (1 - α) · generalization(a)
where α = 0.7 (default, ablation target)
```

- **pass_rate**: Adapter's test-passing rate on held-out tasks of same type
- **generalization**: Cross-task coverage metric (performance on out-of-domain tasks)
- **0.7 weighting**: Prefer specialization over generalization by default

### **Four Operations**

1. **Consolidate**: Merge related adapters (>0.5 similarity) → higher hierarchy level
2. **Update**: Replace lower-fitness adapters with higher-fitness ones
3. **Forget**: Archive adapters below fitness threshold (immutable, non-deletable)
4. **Merge**: TIES-merge top-3 adapters per task type (ablation target — may be disabled)

### **Why Evolution Addresses the Composition Problem**

From **Zhang et al., Zou et al.** (Background section):
- Orthogonal adapter subspaces ≠ semantic coherence
- Merging adapters can reactivate unwanted latent traces
- Naive composition doesn't guarantee beneficial behavior

**Rune's solution**:
- Test fitness empirically before promotion
- Use fitness-driven selection, not assumed composition safety
- Default: single-adapter selection (specify → load → infer)
- Composition: only if empirical evidence shows higher fitness

---

## 8. COMPLETE TRAINING PIPELINE SUMMARY

### **Data Flow End-to-End**

```
AGENT LOOP (generate-execute-reflect cycle)
  ↓
TRAJECTORY RECORDING
  task_description, generated_code, stdout/stderr, tests_passed
  → saved to ~/.rune/trajectories/{session_id}.json
  ↓
STAGE 1: QLORA BOOTSTRAP
  load_trajectory(session_id)
  → format_for_sft() → [system, user, assistant] messages
  → SFTTrainer on NF4-quantized Qwen3.5-9B
  → warm-start from DeltaCoder
  → produces rank-64 adapter
  → register in SQLite with fitness_score=None (unevaluated)
  ↓
STAGE 2: HYPERNETWORK TRAINING
  collect 50-100 Stage 1 adapters
  → for each trajectory:
     - extract activations (base model forward)
     - pass through Sakana perceiver aggregator
     - train weight_head to reconstruct Stage 1 adapter weights
     - KL-divergence distillation loss: α·KL(student||teacher) + (1-α)·CE
  → produces trained hypernetwork checkpoint
  ↓
INFERENCE: HYPERNETWORK GENERATION
  new trajectory → tokenize → base model activation extraction
  → hypernetwork forward pass → rank-8 adapter (milliseconds)
  → load into base model via PEFT
  → generate code with trajectory-conditioned behavior
  ↓
EVOLUTION SWEEP (periodic)
  query best adapters per task_type
  → compute fitness φ(a) for each
  → TIES-merge top-3 (if beneficial)
  → archive low-fitness adapters
  → promote high-fitness merged adapters
  ↓
CONTINUOUS IMPROVEMENT
  High-fitness adapters → retrain hypernetwork
  New trajectories → new Stage 1 adapters → evolve corpus
```

---

## 9. KEY QUANTITATIVE FINDINGS

From **Report_2_LoRA_Fine_Tuning_Strategy.md** and **Training_Review2.md**:

| Parameter | Value | Rationale |
|-----------|-------|-----------|
| **LoRA Rank (Stage 1)** | 64 | Matches DeltaCoder warm-start |
| **LoRA Rank (Stage 2)** | 8 | Sakana checkpoint default; sweep to 16–32 |
| **Scaling Factor** | 0.16× | Bayesian-optimized (200 trials); Sakana default 45.25× too aggressive |
| **KL Blend (α)** | 0.5 | Equal weight to KL divergence and CE loss in distillation |
| **Trajectory Corpus** | 50–100 | Sufficient for hypernetwork transfer learning |
| **Temperature** | 0.25 (prev) → TBD | Re-optimize on Qwen3.5-9B stack |
| **Kill-switch Gate** | ≥5% Pass@1 | Improvement over baseline on held-out HumanEval tasks |
| **Expected Range** | 5–15% | 15%+ should trigger data leakage check |

---

## 10. CRITICAL IMPLEMENTATION GAPS & RISKS

From **Training_Review.md** (Section 3: Edge Cases and Robustness):

### **High-Priority Issues**

1. **Trajectory Recording Disconnected**
   - QLoRA path expects `~/.rune/trajectories/{session_id}.json`
   - `rune_runner.py` never calls `record_trajectory()`
   - **Impact**: QLoRA training has no real data, falls back to synthetic

2. **DocToLoraHypernetwork Weight Head Too Large**
   - Single linear layer: 8,192 → 25M+ parameters
   - Larger than target adapters it generates
   - **Impact**: Impractical for production; needs factorization

3. **CRC32 Tokenizer Hash Collisions**
   - `trajectory_to_tokens` uses `zlib.crc32(trigram) % vocab_size`
   - 32-bit CRC → 50% collision probability at ~7.7k trigrams
   - **Impact**: Semantically different trajectories produce identical tokens

4. **Causal LM Loss Misalignment (D2L)**
   - Answer span slicing doesn't account for token shift
   - Off-by-one errors in loss computation
   - **Impact**: Model trains on wrong token positions

5. **Concurrent SQLite Access Contention**
   - No row-level locking in SQLite
   - Multiple training workers → SQLITE_BUSY errors
   - **Impact**: Non-deterministic training failures

---

## SUMMARY: WHAT TRAJECTORIES TEACH ADAPTERS

**Trajectories are the richest procedural representation available:**

1. **Temporal ordering** teaches causality (what-follows-what)
2. **Code execution traces** teach concrete semantics (what-actually-happens)
3. **Error messages** teach failure patterns (what-goes-wrong)
4. **Fixes** teach recovery strategies (how-to-fix-it)
5. **Attempt sequences** teach iteration and refinement

**Adapters encode this as parameter deltas** that modify the base model's behavior without requiring prompt context. The **0.16× scaling** ensures procedural patterns augment (not override) the base model's reasoning. The **two-step diagnose→repair** pattern ensures adapters learn both pattern recognition and correction. The **three-level hierarchy** ensures knowledge composes from task-specific to domain-general scales.

**This is fundamentally different from RAG** because the knowledge is inscribed in the model's parameters, not retrieved from external storage. It's composable, scalable, and carries the full procedural semantics of the original trajectory.
