---

## Implementation Plan: GitHub Training Data Mining Pipeline

### 1. Summary and Architectural Overview

The goal is to fill in the two `NotImplementedError` stubs in `d2l_mining.py` and create a normalization bridge so that GitHub-mined trajectory data flows cleanly through the existing KL-divergence context distillation pipeline. The pipeline consumes `activation_text` (trajectory context without the answer) and `teacher_text` (trajectory context with the answer), where the hypernetwork maps activation_text's per-layer activations into LoRA weight matrices, and the loss compares teacher output (sees full text) against student output (uses the generated adapter but only sees the prompt).

The existing plan at `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-03-31-data-mining-implementation.md` provides a solid foundation. My analysis below identifies specific architectural decisions it gets right, places where it falls short or introduces risk, and the precise design I recommend.

---

### 2. Design Decisions (Answering All Nine Questions)

**Q1: Trajectory text rendering**

Do not create a separate `render_mined_trajectory()` function. Instead, adapt the step schema so mined data works with the existing `format_for_distillation()` via a single `normalize_mined_trajectory()` bridge function in `d2l_data.py`. This keeps one rendering path rather than two, avoiding drift.

The existing `format_for_distillation()` at line 283-337 of `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_data.py` builds `activation_text` from step `description` fields joined by newlines (lines 308-314), then appends `task_description`. For teacher_text, it appends `canonical_solution` from the step with `tests_passed=True` (lines 321-336). The normalization function must map mined steps to this schema exactly.

**Q2: Quality filtering**

PRs with review cycles (at least one review comment) are more valuable than drive-by merges because they contain the self-correcting structure (attempt, feedback, revision) that creates richer activation patterns. Similarly, issues with multiple fixing commits show iterative refinement.

However, filtering should happen in the mining functions themselves, not in the normalization layer. The existing plan does not filter -- it processes all PRs/issues returned. I recommend adding a `min_review_comments` parameter (default 0 to preserve the existing signature) and a `min_commits` parameter (default 1). For the initial implementation, fetch everything and let the normalization bridge's outcome mapping (`closed`/`open` maps to `failure`, which `format_for_distillation` already drops) handle the coarse filtering. Quality filtering can be layered on top in a subsequent iteration without changing the API.

**Q3: Outcome normalization**

Map as follows:
- `"merged"` maps to `"success"` -- this is the only outcome that produces training records.
- `"closed"` (unmerged PR) maps to `"failure"` -- format_for_distillation returns empty list.
- `"open"` maps to `"failure"` -- same filtering.

For issue chains specifically, `"closed"` means the issue was resolved, which IS a success. This is a critical distinction the existing plan misses. The normalization function should check the `task_id` prefix:
- `task_id` starting with `"pr_"`: `"merged"` is success, everything else is failure.
- `task_id` starting with `"issue_"`: `"closed"` is success, everything else is failure.

**Q4: Step schema mapping**

Mining produces steps with `{type: "commit"/"review", description: str, content: str}`. The distillation pipeline expects `{description: str, generated_code: str, tests_passed: bool, canonical_solution: str}`.

The mapping in `normalize_mined_trajectory()`:
- `description`: Concatenate the step type label with the original description. For commits: `"[Commit] {commit_message}"`. For reviews: `"[Review] {comment_body}"`. This produces richer activation text since `format_for_distillation` joins descriptions with newlines.
- `generated_code`: Set to `content` for commit steps. Set to empty string for review steps (reviews have no code). This field is only used when `tests_passed=True`.
- `tests_passed`: Only `True` for the final commit step in a successful trajectory.
- `canonical_solution`: Only set on the final commit step of a successful trajectory. This is what becomes the "answer" in teacher_text.

**Q5: The "answer" extraction**

For PR chains, the answer should be the **final commit's patch** (not the cumulative diff). The final commit represents the converged solution after all review iterations. The cumulative diff loses the iterative structure that makes the activation text valuable -- the whole point is that intermediate steps (earlier commits + review comments) form the trajectory context, and the final commit is the distilled solution.

For issue chains, the answer should be the **last fixing commit's patch**. If there are multiple commits referencing the same issue, they represent iterative attempts. The last one is the resolution.

This is what the existing plan implements (marking only the last commit step as `tests_passed=True` with `canonical_solution`), and it is correct.

**Q6: Rich step descriptions**

Step descriptions need to be informative because they become the activation text that the hypernetwork processes. For commit steps, use the full commit message (not truncated). For review comment steps, include the full comment body as the description. The existing plan's mining implementation already does this -- commit steps use `commit["commit"]["message"]` as description, review steps use `"Review comment"` as description with the body as content.

However, there is a subtlety: for activation text quality, review step descriptions should include the comment body inline rather than relegating it to the `content` field. This is because `format_for_distillation` only uses `description` for building the trajectory text (lines 308-313 of d2l_data.py). So in `normalize_mined_trajectory`, review steps should set `description` to `"[Review] {content}"` and `generated_code` to `""`.

**Q7: Diff rendering**

Use full unified diffs with file path annotations. The existing plan's format (`"--- {filename} ---\n{patch}"`) is adequate. Full diffs preserve context (surrounding lines, hunk headers) which gives the hypernetwork more signal about what changed and why. The doc-to-lora research shows contexts up to 40K tokens work despite training on under 2,344 tokens, so length is not a concern for the activation extraction pass.

**Q8: GitHub API helper**

Create `github_client.py` as a standalone module within model-training. Key design points:
- Use synchronous `httpx.get()` (not async). Mining runs on a training VM as a batch job, not in an event loop.
- Add `httpx>=0.28.0` to model-training's `pyproject.toml` dependencies. It is already a root workspace dependency at `>=0.28.1`.
- Rate-limit retry: detect 403 with `"rate limit"` in body, respect `Retry-After` header, fallback to 60s.
- Pagination: follow `Link: <url>; rel="next"` headers with a configurable `max_pages` limit.
- Timeout: 30s per request.

**Q9: Testing strategy**

All tests must mock HTTP at the `GitHubClient` class level. The mining tests mock `GitHubClient` as a whole class (using `@patch("model_training.d2l_mining.GitHubClient")`), while the client tests mock `httpx.get` directly.

The existing plan uses `unittest.mock.patch` which is the correct approach. `respx` (mentioned in the plan doc line 9) is not a dependency and should not be added -- `unittest.mock` is sufficient and consistent with the existing test patterns in `test_d2l_data.py` (which uses `MagicMock` extensively).

---

### 3. Precise File-by-File Implementation Plan

#### File 1: `libs/model-training/pyproject.toml`

Add `httpx>=0.28.0` to the `dependencies` list. Currently at line 7-11:
```
dependencies = [
    "shared",
    "adapter-registry",
    "mlflow>=3.0.0",
]
```
Should become:
```
dependencies = [
    "shared",
    "adapter-registry",
    "mlflow>=3.0.0",
    "httpx>=0.28.0",
]
```

#### File 2: `libs/model-training/src/model_training/github_client.py` (new file)

A `GitHubClient` class with:
- `__init__(self, token: str | None = None, base_url: str = "https://api.github.com")` -- sets auth headers
- `get(self, path: str, params: dict | None = None, max_retries: int = 3) -> Any` -- single-endpoint GET with rate-limit retry
- `get_paginated(self, path: str, params: dict | None = None, max_pages: int = 10, per_page: int = 100) -> list[Any]` -- follows Link-next headers

The implementation in the existing plan doc (lines 186-299) is correct and ready to use with one minor adjustment: the `get` method should handle the case where `resp.json()` raises on a non-JSON 403 body. Wrap the `"rate limit" in resp.json().get("message", "").lower()` check in a try/except.

#### File 3: `libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_mining.py` (replace stubs)

Replace the entire file. Key changes:
- Import `GitHubClient` from `model_training.github_client`
- Import `math` and `re` for the fixes pattern
- Define `_FIXES_RE = re.compile(r"(?:fix(?:es)?|close[sd]?|resolve[sd]?)\s+#(\d+)", re.IGNORECASE)`
- `mine_pr_diff_chains`: fetches closed PRs, for each PR fetches commits and review comments, builds step list with type/description/content
- `mine_issue_commit_chains`: fetches issues (filters out PRs), fetches recent commits, scans messages with `_FIXES_RE`, builds linked map, fetches commit details

One correction to the existing plan's `mine_issue_commit_chains`: the issue body needs to include the title as well, not just the body field. Many issues have short or empty bodies but descriptive titles. Use `f"{title}\n\n{body}".strip()` like the PR function does.

#### File 4: `libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_data.py` (add normalization)

Add `normalize_mined_trajectory()` function after `format_for_distillation()`. Add it to `__all__`.

**Critical correction to the existing plan**: for issue-chain trajectories (task_id starting with `"issue_"`), outcome `"closed"` should map to `"success"`, not `"failure"`. The normalization logic should be:

```python
def normalize_mined_trajectory(mined: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    outcome = mined.get("outcome", "")
    task_id = mined.get("task_id", "")
    
    if task_id.startswith("pr_"):
        normalized_outcome = "success" if outcome == "merged" else "failure"
    elif task_id.startswith("issue_"):
        normalized_outcome = "success" if outcome == "closed" else "failure"
    else:
        normalized_outcome = "success" if outcome in ("merged", "closed") else "failure"
    
    # ... step conversion logic ...
```

The step conversion logic should use the enriched description format:
- Commit steps: `description = f"[Commit] {step_description}"`, `generated_code = step_content`
- Review steps: `description = f"[Review] {step_content}"`, `generated_code = ""`

Only the last commit step gets `tests_passed=True` and `canonical_solution`.

#### File 5: `libs/model-training/tests/test_github_client.py` (new file)

Six tests as laid out in the existing plan:
1. Auth header set when token provided
2. Auth header absent when no token
3. `get()` returns parsed JSON
4. `get_paginated()` follows Link-next headers
5. `get_paginated()` respects max_pages
6. Rate-limit retry on 403

All mock `httpx.get` directly.

#### File 6: `libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_mining.py` (new file)

Two test classes:
- `TestMinePrDiffChains` (6 tests): required fields, closed-unmerged outcome, commit diff content, review comment content, empty repo, max_prs limit
- `TestMineIssueCommitChains` (5 tests): required fields, fixes/closes/resolves pattern matching, open issue outcome, no-linked-commits skip, PR-issues excluded

All mock `GitHubClient` class at the `d2l_mining` module level.

#### File 7: `libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_data.py` (append 3 tests)

- Test 11: `normalize_mined_trajectory` maps merged PR to `outcome="success"` with proper step conversion
- Test 12: `normalize_mined_trajectory` maps closed unmerged PR to `outcome="failure"`
- Test 13: End-to-end: normalized merged-PR trajectory produces distillation records via `format_for_distillation`

**Add Test 14**: `normalize_mined_trajectory` maps closed issue to `outcome="success"` (this is missing from the existing plan).

#### File 8: `scripts/mine_github.py` (new file)

CLI wrapper that calls mining functions, optionally normalizes, and writes JSON output. Standard argparse with `--repo`, `--mode`, `--max`, `--token`, `-o`, `--normalize` flags. Uses `GITHUB_TOKEN` env var fallback.

---

### 4. Data Flow Diagram

```
GitHub API
    |
    v
GitHubClient (github_client.py)
    |  .get(), .get_paginated()
    v
mine_pr_diff_chains / mine_issue_commit_chains (d2l_mining.py)
    |
    | produces: {task_id, task_description, steps[{type, description, content}], outcome: "merged"/"closed"/"open"}
    v
normalize_mined_trajectory (d2l_data.py)
    |
    | produces: {task_id, session_id, task_description, steps[{description, generated_code, tests_passed, canonical_solution}], outcome: "success"/"failure"}
    v
format_for_distillation (d2l_data.py)
    |
    | produces: [{task_id, activation_text, teacher_text}]
    v
save_jsonl / prepare_training_jsonl (d2l_data.py / d2l_prep.py)
    |
    | writes: train.jsonl
    v
train_d2l_qwen3 (d2l_train.py)
    |  load_jsonl -> _training_step(record) -> extract_activations(activation_text) -> hypernet -> lora_dict
    v
KL-divergence loss -> backprop through hypernetwork head
```

---

### 5. Potential Risks and Mitigations

1. **activation_text length**: PR diff chains with many commits and review comments may produce very long activation_text. The doc-to-lora research shows up to 40K tokens work, and `d2l_train.py` truncates at `max_length` (default 512 tokens, line 105). For mined data, `max_length` should be increased (2048 or 4096) to avoid discarding the iterative review structure that makes this data valuable. This is a training config change, not a code change.

2. **Issue-closed-as-success vs PR-closed-as-failure**: This distinction is critical. A closed issue means it was resolved; a closed unmerged PR means it was abandoned. The existing plan doc gets this wrong for issues -- it maps `"closed"` to `"failure"` unconditionally. The normalization function must branch on `task_id` prefix.

3. **GitHub API rate limits**: Unauthenticated requests are limited to 60/hour, authenticated to 5000/hour. Mining 100 PRs requires roughly 100 (list) + 100*N_commits (commit details) + 100*1 (review comments) requests. With a token and rate-limit retry, this is manageable. The existing plan's `time.sleep(wait)` approach is correct for a batch job.

4. **Import-time side effects**: The `GitHubClient` imports `httpx` at module level (not deferred). This is acceptable because `httpx` is a pure-Python library with no GPU dependencies, and `d2l_mining.py` is documented as "run on L4 VM, not in CI." However, the tests must mock `httpx` to avoid network calls.

5. **Test isolation**: The mining tests mock `GitHubClient` at the class level (`@patch("model_training.d2l_mining.GitHubClient")`). This means the actual `GitHubClient.__init__` is never called in tests, which is correct. The `get_paginated.side_effect` list approach requires careful ordering since different test methods set up different side_effect sequences.

---

### 6. Implementation Order

| Step | Task | Dependencies |
|------|------|-------------|
| 1 | Add `httpx>=0.28.0` to `libs/model-training/pyproject.toml`, run `uv sync` | None |
| 2 | Create `github_client.py` + `test_github_client.py` | Step 1 |
| 3 | Implement `mine_pr_diff_chains` in `d2l_mining.py` + PR chain tests in `test_d2l_mining.py` | Step 2 |
| 4 | Implement `mine_issue_commit_chains` in `d2l_mining.py` + issue chain tests | Step 2 |
| 5 | Add `normalize_mined_trajectory()` to `d2l_data.py` + tests 11-14 in `test_d2l_data.py` | Steps 3, 4 |
| 6 | Create `scripts/mine_github.py` CLI | Steps 3, 4, 5 |
| 7 | Full test suite validation | All above |

Steps 3 and 4 can be done in parallel since they share the same file but different functions.

---

### Critical Files for Implementation

- `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_data.py`

```
283	def format_for_distillation(trajectory: dict[str, Any]) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
284	    """Convert a trajectory to distillation records with activation/teacher split.
285	
286	    Each record has:
287	    - activation_text: trajectory context + task description (NO answer tokens)
288	    - teacher_text: trajectory context + task description + answer
289	    - task_id: identifier for train/test splitting
290	
291	    Only successful trajectories (outcome == 'success') produce records.
292	
293	    Args:
294	        trajectory: Trajectory dict with task_id/session_id, task_description,
295	            steps, and outcome fields.
296	
297	    Returns:
298	        List of distillation record dicts, or empty list if no successful outcome.
299	    """
300	    if trajectory.get("outcome") != "success":
301	        return []
302	
303	    steps: list[dict[str, Any]] = trajectory.get("steps", [])
304	    task_description: str = trajectory.get("task_description", "")
305	    task_id: str = trajectory.get("task_id") or trajectory.get("session_id", "")
306	
307	    # Build trajectory context from step descriptions (no answer tokens)
308	    trajectory_parts: list[str] = []
309	    for step in steps:
310	        desc = step.get("description", "")
311	        if desc:
312	            trajectory_parts.append(desc)
313	
314	    trajectory_text = "\n".join(trajectory_parts)
315	
316	    # Build activation_text (context only, no answer)
317	    activation_base = f"{trajectory_text}\n{task_description}".strip()
318	
319	    records: list[dict[str, str]] = []
320	    for step in steps:
321	        if not step.get("tests_passed"):
322	            continue
323	
324	        # Extract answer from canonical_solution or generated_code
325	        answer: str = step.get("canonical_solution") or step.get("generated_code", "")
326	        if not answer:
327	            continue
328	
329	        records.append(
330	            {
331	                "task_id": task_id,
332	                "activation_text": activation_base,
333	                "teacher_text": f"{activation_base}\n{answer}",
334	            }
335	        )
336	
337	    return records
```

## Approved Plan:
# GitHub Training Data Mining Implementation Plan

## Context

Rune's hypernetwork (doc-to-lora) generates LoRA adapters from trajectory text via context distillation. The training pipeline works as:

1. **Trajectory text** is tokenized and fed through a frozen base model to extract per-layer activations
2. A **Perceiver-based hypernetwork** maps those activations to LoRA weight matrices
3. **Teacher forward**: base model sees full trajectory + answer (no adapter)
4. **Student forward**: base model with LoRA adapter (from hypernetwork) sees only the prompt (no trajectory context)
5. **Loss** = `alpha * KL(teacher || student) + (1-alpha) * CE(student, ground_truth)` over the answer span
6. Gradients flow back through the hypernetwork head

**The trajectory text IS the document being distilled into adapter weights.** Its quality directly determines what procedural knowledge the adapter encodes. Currently, both mining functions in `d2l_mining.py` raise `NotImplementedError` -- there is no real external training data source.

### Why This Data Structure Matters

Research findings that shape our approach:

- **SWE-bench++**: Three-state (Base/Before/After) approach with fail-to-pass test transitions. Four-layer quality filtering. Hint-guided trajectory synthesis at capability boundaries.
- **Doc-to-LoRA (Sakana)**: Documents chunked 25-256 tokens, contexts up to ~40K work despite training on <2,344 tokens. Perceiver cross-attention maps variable activations to fixed-shape LoRA.
- **OPCD (On-Policy Context Distillation)**: Reverse KL (mode-seeking) on student trajectories outperforms forward KL. Student focuses on teacher's high-probability regions.
- **Rune's own findings**: Skeleton prompts + prose trajectories is the winning combination. Adapter scaling 0.16x (not Sakana's 45.25x). Self-correcting structure (attempt->error->diagnosis->fix) is the key training signal.

### How activation_text/teacher_text Maps to Mined Data

The existing `format_for_distillation()` builds:
- `activation_text` from step `description` fields joined by newlines + task_description
- `teacher_text` = activation_text + `canonical_solution` from the step with `tests_passed=True`

For mined data:
- **activation_text** = task description + iterative process (intermediate commits, review comments) -- the procedural context
- **teacher_text** = activation_text + final solution code (the converged diff)
- The hypernetwork learns: "given this iterative coding process, produce an adapter that predicts the final solution"

---

## File Structure

| File | Action | Purpose |
|------|--------|---------|
| `libs/model-training/pyproject.toml` | Modify | Add `httpx>=0.28.0` dependency |
| `libs/model-training/src/model_training/github_client.py` | Create | GitHub REST API helper: auth, pagination, rate-limit retry |
| `libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_mining.py` | Modify | Fill in `mine_pr_diff_chains` and `mine_issue_commit_chains` stubs |
| `libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_data.py` | Modify | Add `normalize_mined_trajectory()` bridge function |
| `libs/model-training/tests/test_github_client.py` | Create | Tests for GitHubClient (mock httpx) |
| `libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_mining.py` | Create | Tests for both mining functions (mock GitHubClient) |
| `libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_data.py` | Modify | Add tests for normalize_mined_trajectory |
| `scripts/mine_github.py` | Create | CLI runner for mining + normalization |

---

## Data Flow

```
GitHub API
    |
    v
GitHubClient.get_paginated()  (github_client.py)
    |
    v
mine_pr_diff_chains / mine_issue_commit_chains  (d2l_mining.py)
    |
    | Output: {task_id, task_description, steps[{type, description, content}], outcome: "merged"/"closed"/"open"}
    v
normalize_mined_trajectory  (d2l_data.py)
    |
    | Maps: "merged" PR -> "success", "closed" issue -> "success", else -> "failure"
    | Converts steps: {type,description,content} -> {description,generated_code,tests_passed,canonical_solution}
    | Output: distillation-compatible trajectory dict
    v
format_for_distillation  (d2l_data.py, EXISTING - no changes)
    |
    | Output: [{task_id, activation_text, teacher_text}]
    v
prepare_training_jsonl / save_jsonl  (d2l_prep.py, EXISTING - no changes)
    |
    v
train.jsonl -> d2l_train.py -> KL-divergence loss -> hypernetwork head gradients
```

---

## Critical Design Decisions

### 1. Outcome Normalization (PR vs Issue semantics differ)

- **PR "merged"** -> "success" (code was accepted)
- **PR "closed" (unmerged)** -> "failure" (abandoned)
- **Issue "closed"** -> "success" (issue was resolved -- opposite of PR!)
- **Issue "open"** -> "failure" (unresolved)

Branch on `task_id` prefix (`pr_` vs `issue_`) in `normalize_mined_trajectory()`.

### 2. Step Description Enrichment

`format_for_distillation()` builds activation_text from step descriptions only (lines 308-314). Review comment bodies must go INTO the description field, not just the content field, or they'll be invisible to the activation text:

- Commit steps: `description = f"[Commit] {commit_message}"`, `generated_code = diff_patches`
- Review steps: `description = f"[Review] {comment_body}"`, `generated_code = ""`

### 3. "Answer" Extraction

The "answer" (what goes in `canonical_solution` and becomes teacher_text) is the **final commit's patch** -- not the cumulative diff. This preserves the iterative structure: intermediate commits form the context, the final commit is the converged solution.

Only the last commit step gets `tests_passed=True` and `canonical_solution`.

### 4. Quality Filtering

Not all PRs are equally valuable for training. PRs with review cycles contain self-correcting structure (the key training signal). The mining functions will fetch all closed PRs but the normalization layer naturally filters:
- Unmerged PRs -> outcome "failure" -> `format_for_distillation` returns []
- Open issues -> outcome "failure" -> filtered
- PRs with zero commits -> no steps -> no canonical_solution -> no records

Future enhancement: add `min_review_comments` / `min_commits` parameters.

### 5. Diff Rendering

Use full unified diffs with file path annotations: `"--- {filename} ---\n{patch}"`. Full diffs preserve surrounding context (hunk headers, neighboring lines) which gives the hypernetwork more signal. Doc-to-lora handles up to 40K tokens, so length is not a concern.

---

## Task Breakdown

### Task 1: Add httpx dependency
- Modify `libs/model-training/pyproject.toml`: add `"httpx>=0.28.0"` to dependencies
- Run `uv sync --all-extras`

### Task 2: Create GitHubClient
- **Create** `libs/model-training/src/model_training/github_client.py`
- **Create** `libs/model-training/tests/test_github_client.py`
- Class: `GitHubClient(token, base_url)`
- Methods: `get(path, params, max_retries)`, `get_paginated(path, params, max_pages, per_page)`
- Features: Bearer token auth, `Link: <next>` pagination, 403 rate-limit retry with `Retry-After` header
- Sync httpx only (batch job, not async)
- Wrap rate-limit JSON parse in try/except for non-JSON 403 bodies

### Task 3: Implement mine_pr_diff_chains
- **Modify** `libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_mining.py`
- Replace NotImplementedError stub
- For each closed PR: fetch commits + review comments, build steps with type/description/content
- Step format: `{type: "commit"/"review", description: str, content: str}`
- Commit content = file patches joined with `"--- {filename} ---\n{patch}"`
- Outcome: `"merged"` if `merged_at` is not None, else `"closed"`

### Task 4: Implement mine_issue_commit_chains
- Continue modifying `d2l_mining.py`
- Fetch issues (filter out PRs via `pull_request` key), fetch repo commits
- Scan commit messages with `_FIXES_RE` pattern for "fixes #N", "closes #N", "resolves #N"
- Group commits by issue number, build trajectories
- Include issue title + body in task_description (not body alone)
- Skip issues with zero linked commits

### Task 5: Add normalize_mined_trajectory
- **Modify** `libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_data.py`
- Add to `__all__`
- Maps mining format -> distillation format with correct outcome semantics:
  - PR `task_id` prefix: "merged" -> "success"
  - Issue `task_id` prefix: "closed" -> "success"
- Converts steps:
  - Commit: `description="[Commit] {msg}"`, `generated_code=content`, `tests_passed` only on last commit if success
  - Review: `description="[Review] {content}"`, `generated_code=""`, `tests_passed=False`
- Last commit step of successful trajectory gets `canonical_solution = content`

### Task 6: Tests for mining functions
- **Create** `libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_mining.py`
- TestMinePrDiffChains (6 tests): required fields, outcome mapping, commit diffs, review comments, empty repo, max_prs limit
- TestMineIssueCommitChains (5 tests): required fields, fixes/closes/resolves patterns, open outcome, no-linked-commits skip, PR-issues excluded
- All mock `GitHubClient` class at module level

### Task 7: Tests for normalization
- **Modify** `libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_data.py`
- Test 11: merged PR -> outcome "success" with correct step conversion
- Test 12: closed unmerged PR -> outcome "failure"
- Test 13: end-to-end: normalized merged PR produces distillation records
- Test 14: closed issue -> outcome "success" (critical: opposite of PR!)

### Task 8: CLI script
- **Create** `scripts/mine_github.py`
- Flags: `--repo`, `--mode {prs,issues,both}`, `--max`, `--token`/`GITHUB_TOKEN`, `-o`, `--normalize`
- Calls mining functions, optionally normalizes, writes JSON

### Task 9: Final validation
- `uv run pytest libs/model-training/tests/ -v` (all tests pass)
- `uv run mypy libs/model-training/src/` (clean)
- `uv run ruff check libs/model-training/ scripts/mine_github.py` (clean)
- `uv run pytest -x` (full project suite passes)

---

## Key Reusable Functions

- `format_for_distillation()` at `d2l_data.py:283-337` -- NO changes needed, normalization bridge makes mined data compatible
- `save_jsonl()` / `load_jsonl()` at `d2l_data.py:252-280` -- used by prep pipeline
- `prepare_training_jsonl()` at `d2l_prep.py:47-84` -- CLI data prep, works with normalized mined data
- `split_by_task_id()` at `d2l_data.py:545-571` -- train/test split with zero task-ID leakage

2. **Pipeline integration**: `mine_github.py --repo X --normalize -o traj.json` -> `d2l_prep traj.json -o train.jsonl` produces valid JSONL with activation_text/teacher_text fields

{
  "description": "Implement Task 2: GitHubClient",
  "prompt": "You are implementing Task 2: Create GitHubClient + tests\n\n## Task Description\n\nCreate a thin GitHub REST API client with auth, pagination, and rate-limit retry.\n\n**Create** `libs/model-training/src/model_training/github_client.py`:\n- Class: `GitHubClient(token: str | None = None, base_url: str = \"https://api.github.com\")`\n- `__init__`: Sets `_headers` dict with `Accept: application/vnd.github+json` and `X-GitHub-Api-Version: 2022-11-28`. If token provided, add `Authorization: Bearer {token}`.\n- `get(self, path: str, params: dict[str, Any] | None = None, max_retries: int = 3) -> Any`: GET a single endpoint. If response is 403 and body contains \"rate limit\", sleep for `Retry-After` header seconds (default 60) and retry up to max_retries times. Wrap the rate-limit JSON parse in try/except for non-JSON 403 bodies. Use `httpx.get()` with timeout=30.0.\n- `get_paginated(self, path: str, params: dict[str, Any] | None = None, max_pages: int = 10, per_page: int = 100) -> list[Any]`: Follows `Link: <url>; rel=\"next\"` headers. Returns flat list of all items across pages.\n- Use `re.compile(r'<([^>]+)>;\\s*rel=\"next\"')` for Link header parsing.\n- Module-level `__all__ = [\"GitHubClient\"]`\n- Docstrings: Google style\n\n**Create** `libs/model-training/tests/test_github_client.py` with 6 tests:\n\n1. `test_auth_header_set_when_token_provided` - Authorization header is \"Bearer ghp_test123\"\n2. `test_auth_header_absent_when_no_token` - No Authorization key in headers\n3. `test_get_returns_json` - Mock httpx.get, verify parsed JSON returned\n4. `test_get_paginated_follows_link_header` - Two pages: first has Link: next header, second doesn't. Verify both pages' items returned.\n5. `test_get_paginated_respects_max_pages` - Link header always present but max_pages=1 stops after one page\n6. `test_rate_limit_retries_on_403` - First call returns 403 with \"rate limit exceeded\" in body and Retry-After: 0 header, second call succeeds. Verify result from second call.\n\nMock at `model_training.github_client.httpx` level using `@patch`. Create a `_json_response` helper that builds mock httpx.Response objects.\n\n## Context\n\nThis is a new module within `libs/model-training/`. It will be imported by `d2l_mining.py` to fetch data from GitHub's REST API. This is designed for batch mining on a training VM, not CI -- so sync httpx is fine.\n\nThe project uses:\n- Python 3.12, `from __future__ import annotations`\n- Google-style docstrings\n- ruff for linting (line-length 88)\n- mypy with strict-ish config\n- `uv run` for all commands\n\n## Before You Begin\n\nIf you have questions, ask them now.\n\n## Your Job\n\n1. Create the two files exactly as specified\n2. Run tests: `uv run pytest libs/model-training/tests/test_github_client.py -v`\n3. Run lint: `uv run ruff check libs/model-training/src/model_training/github_client.py`\n4. Run format: `uv run ruff format libs/model-training/src/model_training/github_client.py libs/model-training/tests/test_github_client.py`\n5. Commit your work\n6. Self-review and report back\n\nWork from: /Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune\n\n## Report Format\n\nWhen done, report:\n- **Status:** DONE | DONE_WITH_CONCERNS | BLOCKED | NEEDS_CONTEXT\n- What you implemented\n- What you tested and test results\n- Files changed\n- Self-review findings"
}

**What was implemented:**
- `GitHubClient` class in `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/github_client.py` with:
  - Auth headers (`Accept`, `X-GitHub-Api-Version`, optional `Authorization: Bearer`)
  - `get()` with rate-limit 403 retry (parses JSON body for "rate limit", reads `Retry-After` header, try/except for non-JSON 403 bodies)
  - `get_paginated()` with Link `rel="next"` header following, `max_pages` cap, and `per_page` injection
  - Module-level `__all__`, compiled regex, Google-style docstrings

- 6 tests in `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/tests/test_github_client.py` with `_json_response` helper building real `httpx.Response` objects, mocking at `model_training.github_client.httpx` level

**Files changed (committed):**
- `libs/model-training/src/model_training/github_client.py` (new)
- `libs/model-training/tests/test_github_client.py` (new)

**Self-review findings:**
- The `get_paginated` method correctly clears `merged_params` after the first request so Link URLs (which embed query params) don't get doubled-up params -- this is a subtle correctness point that's handled right.
- The `_json_response` helper uses a real `httpx.Response` with a dummy `request` attribute rather than a MagicMock, which is cleaner since `raise_for_status()` requires a real request instance.
- The unreachable lines at the end of `get()` (lines 100-101) exist to satisfy the type checker since the `for` loop could theoretically exit without returning. Marked with `pragma: no cover`.

## Verification Report

### ✅ **Spec Compliant**

All requirements have been implemented correctly:

**`github_client.py` verification:**
- ✅ `__all__ = ["GitHubClient"]` present (line 17)
- ✅ `GitHubClient(token: str | None = None, base_url: str = "https://api.github.com")` (lines 35-39)
- ✅ `_headers` set with `Accept: application/vnd.github+json` and `X-GitHub-Api-Version: 2022-11-28` (lines 47-50)
- ✅ Authorization header conditionally added with `Bearer {token}` format (lines 51-52)
- ✅ `get(path, params, max_retries=3)` signature and implementation (lines 54-101)
  - Rate-limit detection on 403 with `message.lower()` check (line 87)
  - Try/except wrapping for non-JSON 403 responses (lines 82-85)
  - Retry-After header parsing and sleep (lines 88-95)
- ✅ `get_paginated(path, params, max_pages=10, per_page=100)` signature and implementation (lines 103-146)

Replace with full implementations.\n\nKey design:\n- Import `GitHubClient` from `model_training.github_client`\n- Import `math`, `re` \n- Define `_FIXES_RE = re.compile(r\"(?:fix(?:es)?|close[sd]?|resolve[sd]?)\\s+#(\\d+)\", re.IGNORECASE)`\n- Keep `__all__ = [\"mine_pr_diff_chains\", \"mine_issue_commit_chains\"]`\n\n`mine_pr_diff_chains(repo, max_prs=100, github_token=None)`:\n1. Create `GitHubClient(token=github_token)`\n2. Fetch closed PRs: `get_paginated(f\"/repos/{repo}/pulls\", params={\"state\": \"closed\", \"sort\": \"updated\", \"direction\": \"desc\"}, max_pages=ceil(max_prs/100))`\n3. Truncate to `max_prs`\n4. For each PR:\n   - Fetch commits: `get_paginated(f\"/repos/{repo}/pulls/{pr_number}/commits\", max_pages=5)`\n   - Fetch review comments: `get_paginated(f\"/repos/{repo}/pulls/{pr_number}/comments\", max_pages=3)`\n   - For each commit, fetch detail: `get(f\"/repos/{repo}/commits/{sha}\")` to get file patches\n   - Build steps list with `{type: \"commit\", description: commit_message, content: joined_patches}` and `{type: \"review\", description: \"Review comment\", content: comment_body}`\n   - Patch format: `\"--- {filename} ---\\n{patch}\"` joined by newlines\n   - Outcome: `\"merged\"` if `merged_at` is not None, else `\"closed\"`\n   - task_id: `f\"pr_{repo}_{pr_number}\"`\n   - task_description: `f\"{title}\\n\\n{body}\".strip()`\n5. Return empty list if no PRs\n\n## Task 4: Implement mine_issue_commit_chains\n\n`mine_issue_commit_chains(repo, max_issues=100, github_token=None)`:\n1. Create `GitHubClient(token=github_token)`\n2. Fetch issues: `get_paginated(f\"/repos/{repo}/issues\", params={\"state\": \"all\", \"sort\": \"updated\", \"direction\": \"desc\"}, max_pages=ceil(max_issues/100))`\n3. Filter out PRs: skip items where `i.get(\"pull_request\")` is truthy\n4. Truncate to `max_issues`\n5. Build `issue_numbers` set and `issue_map` dict\n6. Fetch recent commits: `get_paginated(f\"/repos/{repo}/commits\", max_pages=10)`\n7. Scan each commit message with `_FIXES_RE.findall(msg)` to find issue references\n8. Group commits by issue number in a `linked` dict\n9. For each issue with linked commits:\n   - Fetch commit detail for each (same as PR function)\n   - Build steps with `{type: \"commit\", description: commit_message, content: joined_patches}`\n   - task_id: `f\"issue_{repo}_{issue_number}\"`\n   - task_description: `f\"{title}\\n\\n{body}\".strip()` (include BOTH title and body, not just body)\n   - outcome: `issue.get(\"state\", \"open\")` (preserves \"closed\"/\"open\" from GitHub)\n10. Skip issues with zero linked commits\n11. Return list of trajectories\n\n## Tests\n\n**Create** `libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_mining.py` with two test classes.\n\nAll tests mock `GitHubClient` at class level: `@patch(\"model_training.d2l_mining.GitHubClient\")`\n\nHelper functions (at module top):\n```python\ndef _make_pr(number=1, title=\"Fix the widget\", body=\"This PR fixes the broken widget.\", state=\"closed\", merged=True):\n    return {\"number\": number, \"title\": title, \"body\": body, \"state\": state, \"merged_at\": \"2026-01-01T00:00:00Z\" if merged else None}\n\ndef _make_commit(sha, message):\n    return {\"sha\": sha, \"commit\": {\"message\": message}}\n\ndef _make_commit_detail(sha, patch):\n    return {\"sha\": sha, \"files\": [{\"filename\": \"widget.py\", \"patch\": patch}]}\n\ndef _make_review_comment(body, created_at=\"2026-01-01T01:00:00Z\"):\n    return {\"body\": body, \"created_at\": created_at}\n\ndef _make_issue(number=1, title=\"Bug in parser\", body=\"The parser crashes on empty input.\", state=\"closed\"):\n    return {\"number\": number, \"title\": title, \"body\": body, \"state\": state, \"pull_request\": None}\n\ndef _make_repo_commit(sha, message):\n    return {\"sha\": sha, \"commit\": {\"message\": message}}\n```\n\n**TestMinePrDiffChains** (6 tests):\n1. `test_returns_trajectory_with_required_fields` - Mock: PRs=[1 merged PR], commits=[1 commit], reviews=[1 comment]. Verify task_id, task_description contains title+body, steps is non-empty list, outcome==\"merged\"\n2. `test_closed_unmerged_pr_has_closed_outcome` - PR with merged=False -> outcome==\"closed\"\n3. `test_steps_contain_commit_diffs` - 2 commits -> 2 commit-type steps with diff content\n4. `test_steps_include_review_comments` - 1 commit + 1 review -> review step with type==\"review\" and body content\n5. `test_empty_repo_returns_empty_list` - No PRs -> empty list\n6. `test_max_prs_limits_results` - 5 PRs available but max_prs=2 -> at most 2 results\n\n**TestMineIssueCommitChains** (5 tests):\n1. `test_returns_trajectory_with_required_fields` - Issue #7, commit with \"fixes #7\" -> task_id, task_description, steps, outcome==\"closed\"\n2. `test_matches_fixes_closes_resolves_patterns` - Commits with \"fixes #5\", \"Closes #5\", \"resolves #5\" all link to issue 5 -> 3 steps\n3. `test_open_issue_has_open_outcome` - Issue with state=\"open\" -> outcome==\"open\"\n4. `test_issue_with_no_linked_commits_is_skipped` - No commits reference the issue -> empty list\n5. `test_pull_request_issues_are_excluded` - Item with pull_request key is skipped, real issue is processed\n\nIMPORTANT for mock side_effects: `get_paginated` is called multiple times with different paths. Use `side_effect` as a list where each element corresponds to a sequential call. For mine_pr_diff_chains:\n- Call 1: list of PRs\n- Then for EACH PR: call for commits, call for review comments\nFor mine_issue_commit_chains:\n- Call 1: list of issues\n- Call 2: list of repo commits\n\n`client.get` (not paginated) is called for individual commit details. Use `side_effect` or `return_value` accordingly.\n\n## Context\n\nThis is the core mining implementation. These functions are designed to run on an L4 VM with GitHub token and network access. **Status:** DONE

**What was implemented:**

- **`libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_mining.py`** -- Replaced the two `NotImplementedError` stubs with full implementations:
  - `mine_pr_diff_chains()` -- Fetches closed PRs via `GitHubClient.get_paginated`, then for each PR fetches commits and review comments, builds trajectory dicts with commit diff steps and review comment steps, determines "merged"/"closed" outcome.
  - `mine_issue_commit_chains()` -- Fetches issues (filtering out PRs), fetches recent repo commits, scans commit messages with `_FIXES_RE` regex to link commits to issues, builds trajectory dicts for issues that have linked commits.
  - Module-level `_FIXES_RE` pattern matching `fix(es)`, `close(s/d)`, `resolve(s/d)` followed by `#N`.

- **`libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_mining.py`** -- 11 tests across two test classes, all mocking `GitHubClient`:
  - `TestMinePrDiffChains` (6 tests): required fields, closed/unmerged outcome, commit diffs in steps, review comments in steps, empty repo, max_prs limiting.
  - `TestMineIssueCommitChains` (5 tests): required fields, regex pattern matching (fixes/closes/resolves), open outcome, skipping unlinked issues, excluding PR items from issues endpoint.

**Test results:** 11/11 passing, 97% branch coverage on `d2l_mining.py`.

**Lint/format:** Clean -- ruff check and ruff format both pass.

**Self-review findings:**
- The 3 partial branch misses (97% coverage) are the `for f in files` loops not exercising the empty-patch branch -- acceptable for unit tests that focus on the happy path.
- `task_description` uses `f"{title}\n\n{body}".strip()` which correctly handles `None` body (converted to `""` via `or ""` guard).
- Both functions share the same commit-detail fetching pattern (fetch + patch assembly). If a third miner is added, this could be extracted to a helper, but for now the duplication is minimal and clear.

{"parentUuid":"d4ed40b5-0ae0-49f5-b67a-31320efc43cb","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"edfc89e7-5836-45c1-83c6-3f97a192a61e","agentId":"ab74172e5fe4465be","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"REDACTED","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Thin GitHub REST API client with auth, pagination, and rate-limit retry.\n2\t\n3\tDesigned for batch data mining on a training VM (sync httpx is fine).\n4\t\"\"\"\n5\t\n6\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n7\t\n8\timport logging\n9\timport re\n10\timport time\n11\tfrom typing import Any\n12\t\n13\timport httpx\n14\t\n15\tlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n16\t\n17\t__all__ = [\"GitHubClient\"]\n18\t\n19\t_LINK_NEXT_RE = re.compile(r'<([^>]+)>;\\s*rel=\"next\"')\n20\t\n21\t\n22\tclass GitHubClient:\n23\t    \"\"\"Minimal GitHub REST API client.\n24\t\n25\t    Handles authentication, paginated list endpoints, and automatic\n26\t    retry on rate-limit 403 responses.\n27\t\n28\t    Args:\n29\t        token: GitHub personal access token. Optional for public\n30\t            endpoints but required for private repos and higher rate\n31\t            limits.\n32\t        base_url: API base URL. Override for GitHub Enterprise.\n33\t    \"\"\"\n34\t\n35\t    def __init__(\n36\t        self,\n37\t        token: str | None = None,\n38\t        base_url: str = \"https://api.github.com\",\n39\t    ) -> None:\n40\t        \"\"\"Initialize the client with optional auth token.\n41\t\n42\t        Args:\n43\t            token: GitHub personal access token.\n44\t            base_url: API base URL.\n45\t        \"\"\"\n46\t        self._base_url = base_url.rstrip(\"/\")\n47\t        self._headers: dict[str, str] = {\n48\t            \"Accept\": \"application/vnd.github+json\",\n49\t            \"X-GitHub-Api-Version\": \"2022-11-28\",\n50\t        }\n51\t        if token is not None:\n52\t            self._headers[\"Authorization\"] = f\"Bearer {token}\"\n53\t\n54\t    def get(\n55\t        self,\n56\t        path: str,\n57\t        params: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n58\t        max_retries: int = 3,\n59\t    ) -> Any:\n60\t        \"\"\"GET a single API endpoint with rate-limit retry.\n61\t\n62\t        Args:\n63\t            path: API path relative to base_url (e.g. ``/repos/owner/repo``).\n64\t            params: Optional query parameters.\n65\t            max_retries: Maximum number of retries on rate-limit 403.\n66\t\n67\t        Returns:\n68\t            Parsed JSON response body.\n69\t\n70\t        Raises:\n71\t            httpx.HTTPStatusError: On non-rate-limit error responses.\n72\t        \"\"\"\n73\t        url = f\"{self._base_url}{path}\"\n74\t        for attempt in range(max_retries + 1):\n75\t            resp = httpx.get(\n76\t                url,\n77\t                headers=self._headers,\n78\t                params=params,\n79\t                timeout=30.0,\n80\t            )\n81\t            if resp.status_code == 403:\n82\t                try:\n83\t                    body = resp.json()\n84\t                except Exception:\n85\t                    body = {}\n86\t                message = body.get(\"message\", \"\")\n87\t                if \"rate limit\" in message.lower() and attempt < max_retries:\n88\t                    wait = int(resp.headers.get(\"Retry-After\", \"60\"))\n89\t                    logger.warning(\n90\t                        \"Rate limited, sleeping %ds (attempt %d/%d)\",\n91\t                        wait,\n92\t                        attempt + 1,\n93\t                        max_retries,\n94\t                    )\n95\t                    time.sleep(wait)\n96\t                    continue\n97\t            resp.raise_for_status()\n98\t            return resp.json()\n99\t        # Unreachable in normal flow, but satisfies type checker.\n100\t        resp.raise_for_status()  # pragma: no cover\n101\t        return resp.json()  # pragma: no cover\n102\t\n103\t    def get_paginated(\n104\t        self,\n105\t        path: str,\n106\t        params: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n107\t        max_pages: int = 10,\n108\t        per_page: int = 100,\n109\t    ) -> list[Any]:\n110\t        \"\"\"GET a paginated list endpoint, following Link rel=next headers.\n111\t\n112\t        Args:\n113\t            path: API path relative to base_url.\n114\t            params: Optional query parameters (``per_page`` is injected).\n115\t            max_pages: Maximum number of pages to fetch.\n116\t            per_page: Items per page (max 100 for most GitHub endpoints).\n117\t\n118\t        Returns:\n119\t            Flat list of all items across all fetched pages.\n120\t        \"\"\"\n121\t        merged_params: dict[str, Any] = dict(params or {})\n122\t        merged_params[\"per_page\"] = per_page\n123\t\n124\t        items: list[Any] = []\n125\t        url: str | None = f\"{self._base_url}{path}\"\n126\t\n127\t        for _ in range(max_pages):\n128\t            if url is None:\n129\t                break\n130\t            resp = httpx.get(\n131\t                url,\n132\t                headers=self._headers,\n133\t                params=merged_params,\n134\t                timeout=30.0,\n135\t            )\n136\t            resp.raise_for_status()\n137\t            items.extend(resp.json())\n138\t\n139\t            # After the first request, params are baked into the Link URL.\n140\t            merged_params = {}\n141\t\n142\t            link = resp.headers.get(\"Link\", \"\")\n143\t            match = _LINK_NEXT_RE.search(link)\n144\t            url = match.group(1) if match else None\n145\t\n146\t        return items\n147\t"}]},"uuid":"4d4f4f97-4714-42a5-ad6d-308cb07563e3","timestamp":"2026-03-31T18:26:10.061Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"d4ed40b5-0ae0-49f5-b67a-31320efc43cb","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune","sessionId":"7dcac314-38c9-499c-ac99-9d28e2a4e020","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"feat/data_mining","slug":"floating-sniffing-fiddle"}

{
  "description": "Implement Task 5: normalization",
  "prompt": "You are implementing Task 5: Add normalize_mined_trajectory + tests\n\n## Task Description\n\nAdd a `normalize_mined_trajectory()` function to `libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_data.py` that converts GitHub-mined trajectory dicts into the format expected by the existing `format_for_distillation()` function.\n\n### Function to add (after the existing `format_for_distillation` function around line 337):\n\n```python\ndef normalize_mined_trajectory(mined: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n```\n\n**Logic:**\n\n1. Determine outcome based on task_id prefix:\n   - If task_id starts with `\"pr_\"`: outcome = `\"success\"` if mined outcome is `\"merged\"`, else `\"failure\"`\n   - If task_id starts with `\"issue_\"`: outcome = `\"success\"` if mined outcome is `\"closed\"`, else `\"failure\"`\n   - Fallback: `\"success\"` if outcome in `(\"merged\", \"closed\")`, else `\"failure\"`\n\n2. Convert steps from mining format to distillation format:\n   - Find all commit-type steps: `commit_steps = [s for s in raw_steps if s.get(\"type\") == \"commit\"]`\n   - For each step in the raw steps:\n     - If type == \"commit\": `description = f\"[Commit] {step_description}\"`, `generated_code = step_content`\n     - If type == \"review\": `description = f\"[Review] {step_content}\"`, `generated_code = \"\"`\n     - `tests_passed`: Only `True` for the LAST commit step AND outcome is \"success\"\n     - `canonical_solution`: Only set on that same last commit step (value = step content)\n\n3. Return:\n```python\n{\n    \"task_id\": mined.get(\"task_id\", \"\"),\n    \"session_id\": mined.get(\"task_id\", \"\"),\n    \"task_description\": mined.get(\"task_description\", \"\"),\n    \"steps\": normalized_steps,\n    \"outcome\": normalized_outcome,\n}\n```\n\n**Also:**\n- Add `\"normalize_mined_trajectory\"` to the `__all__` list at the top of the file (line 20-28)\n- Google-style docstring explaining the mapping\n\n### Tests to append to `libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_data.py`:\n\n**Test 11**: `test_normalize_mined_trajectory_merged_pr_becomes_success`\n- Input: merged PR trajectory with 2 commit steps + 1 review step\n- Verify: outcome == \"success\", task_id preserved, steps have [Commit]/[Review] prefixes\n- Verify: only last commit step has tests_passed=True and canonical_solution\n\n**Test 12**: `test_normalize_mined_trajectory_closed_unmerged_pr_becomes_failure`\n- Input: closed (unmerged) PR trajectory\n- Verify: outcome == \"failure\"\n\n**Test 13**: `test_normalized_mined_trajectory_produces_distillation_records`\n- Input: normalized merged PR trajectory passed through format_for_distillation()\n- Verify: produces at least 1 record with task_id, activation_text, teacher_text\n- Verify: activation_text contains \"[Commit]\" text (from descriptions)\n- Verify: teacher_text extends activation_text\n\n**Test 14**: `test_normalize_mined_trajectory_closed_issue_becomes_success`\n- Input: trajectory with task_id=\"issue_owner/repo_7\", outcome=\"closed\"\n- Verify: outcome == \"success\" (CRITICAL: closed issue = success, opposite of PR!)\n\n## Context\n\nThis is the bridge function that connects the mining output to the existing distillation pipeline. The existing `format_for_distillation()` function (d2l_data.py lines 283-337):\n1. Filters on outcome == \"success\" (returns [] for anything else)\n2. Builds activation_text from step descriptions joined by newlines + task_description\n3. Builds teacher_text by appending canonical_solution from steps with tests_passed=True\n\nSo the normalization must produce steps where:\n- `description` fields contain rich text (will become activation_text)\n- Only the final commit has tests_passed=True with canonical_solution (will become the \"answer\" in teacher_text)\n\nThe existing `__all__` list at lines 20-28 is:\n```python\n__all__ = [\n    \"format_for_distillation\",\n    \"generate_needle_dataset\",\n    \"generate_trajectory_dataset\",\n    \"augment_trajectories\",\n    \"save_jsonl\",\n    \"load_jsonl\",\n    \"split_by_task_id\",\n]\n```\n\n## Your Job\n\n1. Add `normalize_mined_trajectory` to d2l_data.py (function + __all__)\n2. Append 4 tests to test_d2l_data.py\n3. Run tests: `uv run pytest libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_data.py -v`\n4. Run lint: `uv run ruff check libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_data.py libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_data.py`\n5. Run format: `uv run ruff format libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_data.py libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_data.py`\n6. > AGENT

The issue is that `self.get()` returns parsed JSON but `get_paginated()` needs the response headers for Link parsing. I'll extract a `_get_response()` method that both can use.

1	"""Thin GitHub REST API client with auth, pagination, and rate-limit retry.
2	
3	Designed for batch data mining on a training VM (sync httpx is fine).
4	"""
5	
6	from __future__ import annotations
7	
8	import logging
9	import re
10	import time
11	from typing import Any
12	
13	import httpx
14	
15	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
16	
17	__all__ = ["GitHubClient"]
18	
19	_LINK_NEXT_RE = re.compile(r'<([^>]+)>;\s*rel="next"')
20	
21	
22	class GitHubClient:
23	    """Minimal GitHub REST API client.
24	
25	    Handles authentication, paginated list endpoints, and automatic
26	    retry on rate-limit 403 responses.
27	
28	    Args:
29	        token: GitHub personal access token. Optional for public
30	            endpoints but required for private repos and higher rate
31	            limits.
32	        base_url: API base URL. Override for GitHub Enterprise.
33	    """
34	
35	    def __init__(
36	        self,
37	        token: str | None = None,
38	        base_url: str = "https://api.github.com",
39	    ) -> None:
40	        """Initialize the client with optional auth token.
41	
42	        Args:
43	            token: GitHub personal access token.
44	            base_url: API base URL.
45	        """
46	        self._base_url = base_url.rstrip("/")
47	        self._headers: dict[str, str] = {
48	            "Accept": "application/vnd.github+json",
49	            "X-GitHub-Api-Version": "2022-11-28",
50	        }
51	        if token is not None:
52	            self._headers["Authorization"] = f"Bearer {token}"
53	
54	    def get(
55	        self,
56	        path: str,
57	        params: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
58	        max_retries: int = 3,
59	    ) -> Any:
60	        """GET a single API endpoint with rate-limit retry.
61	
62	        Args:
63	            path: API path relative to base_url (e.g. ``/repos/owner/repo``).
64	            params: Optional query parameters.
65	            max_retries: Maximum number of retries on rate-limit 403.
66	
67	        Returns:
68	            Parsed JSON response body.
69	
70	        Raises:
71	            httpx.HTTPStatusError: On non-rate-limit error responses.
72	        """
73	        url = f"{self._base_url}{path}"
74	        for attempt in range(max_retries + 1):
75	            resp = httpx.get(
76	                url,
77	                headers=self._headers,
78	                params=params,
79	                timeout=30.0,
80	            )
81	            if resp.status_code == 403:
82	                try:
83	                    body = resp.json()
84	                except Exception:
85	                    body = {}
86	                message = body.get("message", "")
87	                if "rate limit" in message.lower() and attempt < max_retries:
88	                    wait = int(resp.headers.get("Retry-After", "60"))
89	                    logger.warning(
90	                        "Rate limited, sleeping %ds (attempt %d/%d)",
91	                        wait,
92	                        attempt + 1,
93	                        max_retries,
94	                    )
95	                    time.sleep(wait)
96	                    continue
97	            resp.raise_for_status()
98	            return resp.json()
99	        # Unreachable in normal flow, but satisfies type checker.
100	        resp.raise_for_status()  # pragma: no cover
101	        return resp.json()  # pragma: no cover
102	
103	    def get_paginated(
104	        self,
105	        path: str,
106	        params: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
107	        max_pages: int = 10,
108	        per_page: int = 100,
109	    ) -> list[Any]:
110	        """GET a paginated list endpoint, following Link rel=next headers.
111	
112	        Args:
113	            path: API path relative to base_url.
114	            params: Optional query parameters (``per_page`` is injected).
115	            max_pages: Maximum number of pages to fetch.
116	            per_page: Items per page (max 100 for most GitHub endpoints).
117	
118	        Returns:
119	            Flat list of all items across all fetched pages.
120	        """
121	        merged_params: dict[str, Any] = dict(params or {})
122	        merged_params["per_page"] = per_page
123	
124	        items: list[Any] = []
125	        url: str | None = f"{self._base_url}{path}"
126	
127	        for _ in range(max_pages):
128	            if url is None:
129	                break
130	            resp = httpx.get(
131	                url,
132	                headers=self._headers,
133	                params=merged_params,
134	                timeout=30.0,
135	            )
136	            resp.raise_for_status()
137	            items.extend(resp.json())
138	
139	            # After the first request, params are baked into the Link URL.
140	            merged_params = {}
141	
142	            link = resp.headers.get("Link", "")
143	            match = _LINK_NEXT_RE.search(link)
144	            url = match.group(1) if match else None
145	
146	        return items
147

> AGENT

End-to-end pipeline works perfectly:

1. **Mining**: GitHub API -> raw trajectory dicts with commit diffs and review comments
2. **Normalization**: `merged` -> `success`, step descriptions enriched with `[Commit]`/`[Review]` prefixes
3. **Distillation prep**: `format_for_distillation()` produces `activation_text` (procedural context) and `teacher_text` (context + final diff answer)
4. **teacher_text extends activation_text**: Confirmed — the "answer" is the final commit's unified diff appended to the context

The data is now in the exact format that `d2l_train.py` consumes for KL-divergence context distillation. The activation_text contains the iterative process (commit messages, task description), and the teacher_text adds the final code solution — exactly what the hypernetwork needs to learn "given this coding process, produce an adapter that predicts the converged solution."

> AGENT

**What you're seeing:**

- **Record 0** (`pr_encode/httpx_3773`): A chardet 6.0 compatibility fix. The activation_text has the commit message + PR description (the "process context"). The answer is a unified diff showing the test assertion change. This is a good trajectory — it captures why the change was needed and what was done.

- **Record 1** (`pr_encode/httpx_3730`): A Python version matrix update. Thin context (just "3.11+"), simple diff. Less valuable for training — a future quality filter could prioritize PRs with review comments and multiple commits.

**Pipeline flow:**
```
10 closed PRs fetched → 2 merged (success) + 8 unmerged (failure)
→ format_for_distillation filters to outcome=="success" only
→ 2 training records in train.jsonl
→ Each has activation_text (process) + teacher_text (process + answer diff)
```

The single-commit PRs without review comments are the least valuable. For production mining, targeting repos with active code review (multiple commits + reviewer feedback) will produce richer trajectories with the self-correcting structure the hypernetwork needs.

> DEVELOPER

Do you think we should mine and then filter or filter and then mine? The idea is to end the process with a very high quality corpus?

> AGENT

Filter first, then mine. Here's why:

**API calls are the bottleneck.** Each PR costs 3+ API calls (commits, comments, commit details). Mining 1000 PRs to keep 50 is wasteful — both in rate limit budget and wall-clock time on the training VM.

**GitHub's API supports filtering.** We can use search queries to pre-select high-quality PRs before fetching their details:

```
GET /search/issues?q=repo:{repo} is:pr is:merged review:approved comments:>2
```

This gives us PRs that were:
- Actually merged (not abandoned)
- Reviewed and approved (has review cycle)
- Had discussion (comments > 2 = self-correcting structure)

We can also filter by:
- `linked:issue` (PR resolves an issue — clearer task_description)
- Label filters (`bug`, `feature` — skip docs/CI/dependency bumps)
- File extension patterns (skip PRs that only touch `.md`, `.yml`, `.toml`)

**The quality signals we want are available BEFORE mining:**

| Signal | Available pre-mine? | Why it matters |
|--------|-------------------|---------------|
| Merged status | Yes (search API) | Only success trajectories train |
| Review comment count | Yes (search API) | Self-correcting structure |
| Commit count | Yes (PR list API) | Iterative refinement |
| Changed file types | Requires 1 extra call | Skip non-code PRs |
| Linked issue | Yes (search API) | Better task_description |

**Proposed approach:** Add a `search_quality_prs()` function that uses the GitHub Search API to find candidates, then pass those PR numbers to the existing `mine_pr_diff_chains()`. This way the expensive per-PR mining only runs on pre-qualified candidates.

```
1	"""GitHub trajectory mining for coding session distillation.
2	
3	Mines PR diff chains and issue-commit chains from GitHub repositories,
4	producing trajectory dicts suitable for normalization and distillation.
5	Designed to run on an L4 VM with network access and a GITHUB_TOKEN.
6	"""
7	
8	from __future__ import annotations
9	
10	import logging
11	import math
12	import re
13	from typing import Any
14	
15	from model_training.github_client import GitHubClient
16	
17	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
18	
19	__all__ = ["mine_pr_diff_chains", "mine_issue_commit_chains"]
20	
21	_FIXES_RE = re.compile(
22	    r"(?:fix(?:es)?|close[sd]?|resolve[sd]?)\s+#(\d+)", re.IGNORECASE
23	)
24	
25	
26	def mine_pr_diff_chains(
27	    repo: str,
28	    max_prs: int = 100,
29	    github_token: str | None = None,
30	) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
31	    """Extract PR diff chains from a GitHub repository.
32	
33	    Each chain represents an iterative coding session: initial commit ->
34	    review comments -> revision commits. The resulting trajectory records
35	    capture the back-and-forth of code review as a multi-step improvement
36	    process, suitable for distillation.
37	
38	    Returns trajectory dicts with the following fields:
39	    - task_id: f"pr_{repo}_{pr_number}"
40	    - task_description: PR title concatenated with body text
41	    - steps: list of commit diffs and review comments in chronological order
42	    - outcome: "merged" or "closed" depending on PR final state
43	
44	    Args:
45	        repo: GitHub repository in "owner/repo" format.
46	        max_prs: Maximum number of PRs to process. Defaults to 100.
47	        github_token: Personal access token for GitHub API authentication.
48	
49	    Returns:
50	        List of trajectory dicts representing PR diff chains.
51	    """
52	    client = GitHubClient(token=github_token)
53	
54	    prs = client.get_paginated(
55	        f"/repos/{repo}/pulls",
56	        params={"state": "closed", "sort": "updated", "direction": "desc"},
57	        max_pages=math.ceil(max_prs / 100),
58	    )
59	    prs = prs[:max_prs]
60	
61	    if not prs:
62	        return []
63	
64	    trajectories: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
65	
```

88	            for f in files:
89	                patch = f.get("patch", "")
90	                if patch:
91	                    patches.append(f"--- {f['filename']} ---\n{patch}")
92	            steps.append(
93	                {
94	                    "type": "commit",
95	                    "description": msg,
96	                    "content": "\n".join(patches),
97	                }
98	            )
99	
100	        for comment in reviews:
101	            steps.append(
102	                {
103	                    "type": "review",
104	                    "description": "Review comment",
105	                    "content": comment.get("body", ""),
106	                }
107	            )
108	
109	        outcome = "merged" if pr.get("merged_at") is not None else "closed"
110	
111	        trajectories.append(
112	            {
113	                "task_id": f"pr_{repo}_{pr_number}",
114	                "task_description": f"{title}\n\n{body}".strip(),
115	                "steps": steps,
116	                "outcome": outcome,
117	            }
118	        )
119	
120	    return trajectories
121	
122	
123	def mine_issue_commit_chains(
124	    repo: str,
125	    max_issues: int = 100,
126	    github_token: str | None = None,
127	) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
128	    """Link GitHub issues to their fixing commits via commit message references.
129	
130	    Scans commit messages for "fixes #N", "closes #N", or "resolves #N"
131	    patterns to identify which commits address which issues. Groups linked
132	    commits as trajectory steps for distillation.
133	
134	    Returns trajectory dicts with the following fields:
135	    - task_id: f"issue_{repo}_{issue_number}"
136	    - task_description: issue title concatenated with body text
137	    - steps: list of commits referencing this issue in chronological order
138	    - outcome: "closed" or "open" from the issue state
139	
140	    Args:
141	        repo: GitHub repository in "owner/repo" format.
142	        max_issues: Maximum number of issues to process. Defaults to 100.
143	        github_token: Personal access token for GitHub API authentication.
144	
145	    Returns:
146	        List of trajectory dicts representing issue-commit chains.
147	    """
148	    client = GitHubClient(token=github_token)
149	
150	    raw_issues = client.get_paginated(
151	        f"/repos/{repo}/issues",
152	        params={"state": "all", "sort": "updated", "direction": "desc"},
153	        max_pages=math.ceil(max_issues / 100),
154	    )
155	
156	    # Filter out pull requests (GitHub issues API includes PRs)
157	    issues = [i for i in raw_issues if not i.get("pull_request")]
158	    issues = issues[:max_issues]
159	
160	    issue_numbers = {i["number"] for i in issues}
161	    issue_map = {i["number"]: i for i in issues}
162	
163	    repo_commits = client.get_paginated(
164	        f"/repos/{repo}/commits",
165	        max_pages=10,
166	    )
167	
168	    # Group commits by referenced issue number
169	    linked: dict[int, list[dict[str, Any]]] = {}
170	    for commit in repo_commits:
171	        msg = commit["commit"]["message"]
172	        refs = _FIXES_RE.findall(msg)
173	        for ref in refs:
174	            issue_num = int(ref)
175	            if issue_num in issue_numbers:
176	                linked.setdefault(issue_num, []).append(commit)
177	
178	    trajectories: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
179	
180	    for issue_num, commits in linked.items():
181	        issue = issue_map[issue_num]
182	        title = issue.get("title", "")
183	        body = issue.get("body", "") or ""
184	
185	        steps: list[dict[str, str]] = []
186	        for commit in commits:
187	            sha = commit["sha"]
188	            msg = commit["commit"]["message"]
189	            detail = client.get(f"/repos/{repo}/commits/{sha}")
190	            files = detail.get("files", [])
191	            patches = []
192	            for f in files:
193	                patch = f.get("patch", "")
194	                if patch:
195	                    patches.append(f"--- {f['filename']} ---\n{patch}")
196	            steps.append(
197	                {
198	                    "type": "commit",
199	                    "description": msg,
200	                    "content": "\n".join(patches),
201	                }
202	            )
203	
204	        trajectories.append(
205	            {
206	                "task_id": f"issue_{repo}_{issue_num}",
207	                "task_description": f"{title}\n\n{body}".strip(),
208	                "steps": steps,
209	                "outcome": issue.get("state", "open"),
210	            }
211	        )
212	
213	    return trajectories
214

1	"""Thin GitHub REST API client with auth, pagination, and rate-limit retry.
2	
3	Designed for batch data mining on a training VM (sync httpx is fine).
4	"""
5	
6	from __future__ import annotations
7	
8	import logging
9	import re
10	import time
11	from typing import Any
12	
13	import httpx
14	
15	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
16	
17	__all__ = ["GitHubClient"]
18	
19	_LINK_NEXT_RE = re.compile(r'<([^>]+)>;\s*rel="next"')
20	
21	
22	class GitHubClient:
23	    """Minimal GitHub REST API client.
24	
25	    Handles authentication, paginated list endpoints, and automatic
26	    retry on rate-limit 403 responses.
27	
28	    Args:
29	        token: GitHub personal access token. Optional for public
30	            endpoints but required for private repos and higher rate
31	            limits.
32	        base_url: API base URL. Override for GitHub Enterprise.
33	    """
34	
35	    def __init__(
36	        self,
37	        token: str | None = None,
38	        base_url: str = "https://api.github.com",
39	    ) -> None:
40	        """Initialize the client with optional auth token.
41	
42	        Args:
43	            token: GitHub personal access token.
44	            base_url: API base URL.
45	        """
46	        self._base_url = base_url.rstrip("/")
47	        self._headers: dict[str, str] = {
48	            "Accept": "application/vnd.github+json",
49	            "X-GitHub-Api-Version": "2022-11-28",
50	        }
51	        if token is not None:
52	            self._headers["Authorization"] = f"Bearer {token}"
53	
54	    def _get_response(
55	        self,
56	        url: str,
57	        params: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
58	        max_retries: int = 3,
59	    ) -> httpx.Response:
60	        """GET with rate-limit retry, returning the raw response.
61	
62	        Args:
63	            url: Full URL to request.
64	            params: Optional query parameters.
65	            max_retries: Maximum number of retries on rate-limit 403.
66	
67	        Returns:
68	            The successful httpx.Response.
69	
70	        Raises:
71	            httpx.HTTPStatusError: On non-rate-limit error responses.
72	        """
73	        for attempt in range(max_retries + 1):
74	            resp = httpx.get(
75	                url,
76	                headers=self._headers,
77	                params=params,
78	                timeout=30.0,
79	            )
80	            if resp.status_code == 403:
81	                try:
82	                    body = resp.json()
83	                except Exception:
84	                    body = {}
85	                message = body.get("message", "")
86	                if "rate limit" in message.lower() and attempt < max_retries:
87	                    wait = int(resp.headers.get("Retry-After", "60"))
88	                    logger.warning(
89	                        "Rate limited, sleeping %ds (attempt %d/%d)",
90	                        wait,
91	                        attempt + 1,
92	                        max_retries,
93	                    )
94	                    time.sleep(wait)
95	                    continue
96	            resp.raise_for_status()
97	            return resp
98	        # Unreachable in normal flow, but satisfies type checker.
99	        resp.raise_for_status()  # pragma: no cover
100	        return resp  # pragma: no cover
101	
102	    def get(
103	        self,
104	        path: str,
105	        params: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
106	        max_retries: int = 3,
107	    ) -> Any:
108	        """GET a single API endpoint with rate-limit retry.
109	
110	        Args:
111	            path: API path relative to base_url (e.g. ``/repos/owner/repo``).
112	            params: Optional query parameters.
113	            max_retries: Maximum number of retries on rate-limit 403.
114	
115	        Returns:
116	            Parsed JSON response body.
117	
118	        Raises:
119	            httpx.HTTPStatusError: On non-rate-limit error responses.
120	        """
121	        url = f"{self._base_url}{path}"
122	        return self._get_response(url, params=params, max_retries=max_retries).json()
123	
124	    def get_paginated(
125	        self,
126	        path: str,
127	        params: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
128	        max_pages: int = 10,
129	        per_page: int = 100,
130	    ) -> list[Any]:
131	        """GET a paginated list endpoint, following Link rel=next headers.
132	
133	        Args:
134	            path: API path relative to base_url.
135	            params: Optional query parameters (``per_page`` is injected).
136	            max_pages: Maximum number of pages to fetch.
137	            per_page: Items per page (max 100 for most GitHub endpoints).
138	
139	        Returns:
140	            Flat list of all items across all fetched pages.
141	        """
142	        merged_params: dict[str, Any] = dict(params or {})
143	        merged_params["per_page"] = per_page
144	
145	        items: list[Any] = []
146	        url: str | None = f"{self._base_url}{path}"
147	
148	        for _ in range(max_pages):
149	            if url is None:
150	                break
151	            resp = self._get_response(url, params=merged_params)
152	            items.extend(resp.json())
153	
154	            # After the first request, params are baked into the Link URL.
155	            merged_params = {}
156	
157	            link = resp.headers.get("Link", "")
158	            match = _LINK_NEXT_RE.search(link)
159	            url = match.group(1) if match else None
160	
161	        return items
162

1	"""GitHub trajectory mining CLI.
2	
3	Mines GitHub repositories into trajectory JSON files suitable for
4	Doc-to-LoRA distillation. Extracts PR diff chains and/or issue-commit
5	chains and optionally normalizes them for the distillation pipeline.
6	
7	Usage:
8	    uv run python scripts/mine_github.py --repo owner/repo -o out.json
9	    uv run python scripts/mine_github.py --repo owner/repo --mode prs --max 50 -o prs.json
10	    uv run python scripts/mine_github.py --repo owner/repo --mode issues --normalize -o issues.json
11	    uv run python scripts/mine_github.py --repo owner/repo --token ghp_xxx -o both.json
12	"""
13	
14	from __future__ import annotations
15	
16	import argparse
17	import json
18	import logging
19	import os
20	import sys
21	from pathlib import Path
22	
23	logging.basicConfig(
24	    level=logging.INFO,
25	    format="%(asctime)s %(levelname)s %(name)s: %(message)s",
26	)
27	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
28	
29	
30	def parse_args() -> argparse.Namespace:
31	    """Parse command-line arguments."""
32	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
33	        description="Mine a GitHub repository into trajectory JSON files.",
34	        formatter_class=argparse.ArgumentDefaultsHelpFormatter,
35	    )
36	    parser.add_argument(
37	        "--repo",
38	        required=True,
39	        metavar="OWNER/REPO",
40	        help='GitHub repository in "owner/repo" format.',
41	    )
42	    parser.add_argument(
43	        "--mode",
44	        choices=["prs", "issues", "both"],
45	        default="both",
46	        help="Mining mode: pull requests, issues, or both.",
47	    )
48	    parser.add_argument(
49	        "--max",
50	        type=int,
51	        default=100,
52	        dest="max_items",
53	        metavar="N",
54	        help="Maximum PRs/issues to process.",
55	    )
56	    parser.add_argument(
57	        "--token",
58	        default=None,
59	        metavar="TOKEN",
60	        help="GitHub personal access token (falls back to GITHUB_TOKEN env var).",
61	    )
62	    parser.add_argument(
63	        "-o",
64	        "--output",
65	        required=True,
66	        type=Path,
67	        metavar="FILE",
68	        help="Output JSON file path.",
69	    )
70	    parser.add_argument(
71	        "--normalize",
72	        action="store_true",
73	        help="Normalize trajectories for distillation compatibility.",
74	    )
75	    return parser.parse_args()
76	
77	
78	def main() -> None:
79	    """Entry point for the mine_github CLI."""
80	    args = parse_args()
81	
82	    # Resolve GitHub token
83	    token: str | None = args.token or os.environ.get("GITHUB_TOKEN")
84	    if not token:
85	        logger.error(
86	            "No GitHub token provided. Use --token or set the GITHUB_TOKEN env var."
87	        )
88	        sys.exit(1)
89	
90	    from model_training.d2l_data import normalize_mined_trajectory
91	    from model_training.d2l_mining import mine_issue_commit_chains, mine_pr_diff_chains
92	
93	    trajectories: list[dict] = []
94	
95	    # Mine PR diff chains
96	    if args.mode in ("prs", "both"):
97	        logger.info(
98	            "Mining PR diff chains from %s (max=%d)...", args.repo, args.max_items
99	        )
100	        pr_trajectories = mine_pr_diff_chains(
101	            args.repo,
102	            max_prs=args.max_items,
103	            github_token=token,
104	        )
105	        logger.info("Mined %d PR diff chain(s).", len(pr_trajectories))
106	        trajectories.extend(pr_trajectories)
107	
108	    # Mine issue-commit chains
109	    if args.mode in ("issues", "both"):
110	        logger.info(
111	            "Mining issue-commit chains from %s (max=%d)...", args.repo, args.max_items
112	        )
113	        issue_trajectories = mine_issue_commit_chains(
114	            args.repo,
115	            max_issues=args.max_items,
116	            github_token=token,
117	        )
118	        logger.info("Mined %d issue-commit chain(s).", len(issue_trajectories))
119	        trajectories.extend(issue_trajectories)
120	
121	    logger.info("Total trajectories collected: %d.", len(trajectories))
122	
123	    # Normalize if requested
124	    if args.normalize:
125	        logger.info("Normalizing %d trajectory/trajectories...", len(trajectories))
126	        trajectories = [normalize_mined_trajectory(t) for t in trajectories]
127	        logger.info("Normalization complete.")
128	
129	    # Write output
130	    output: Path = args.output
131	    output.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
132	    with output.open("w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
133	        json.dump(trajectories, fh, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)
134	    logger.info("Wrote %d trajectory/trajectories to %s.", len(trajectories), output)
135	
136	
137	if __name__ == "__main__":
138	    main()
139
