After two simple text searches? Huh? · community · Discussion #141073","url":"https://github.com/orgs/community/discussions/141073"},{"title":"Why do I encounter a rate limit error when I have remaining API calls on GitHub? - Github - Latenode Official Community","url":"https://community.latenode.com/t/why-do-i-encounter-a-rate-limit-error-when-i-have-remaining-api-calls-on-github/25029"}]

Based on the search results, here's the comparison between GitHub's Search API rate limits and REST API rate limits for pulls/issues:

## Rate Limit Comparison

**Search API Rate Limits:**
The search API has a limit of 30 requests per minute per Personal Access Token (PAT), which is significantly more restrictive than the core REST API.

**REST API Rate Limits (for pulls/issues and other core endpoints):**
All requests to core endpoints count towards your personal rate limit of 5,000 requests per hour when authenticated with a personal access token.

## Key Differences

Some endpoints, like the search endpoints, have more restrictive limits compared to standard REST API endpoints. This means:

- **Search endpoints**: 30 requests per minute (~1,800 per hour)
- **Core REST API** (including pulls/issues): 5,000 requests per hour

The REST API for searching items has a custom rate limit that is separate from the rate limit governing the other REST API endpoints.

## Additional Information

For code-specific searches, the limits are even stricter. According to the search results, the code_search object provides your rate limit status for the REST API for searching code, with notably lower limits than general search endpoints.

If you need higher rate limits, you should build a GitHub App, as GitHub Apps can be installed by multiple users, and the rate limit is applied per installation—if 1,000 users install your app, you get 30,000 requests per minute.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/noahdolevelixir/.claude/plans/floating-sniffing-fiddle.md",
  "content": "# Quality-First GitHub Mining Filter\n\n## Context\n\nThe current mining implementation fetches all closed PRs, then mines each one (3+ API calls per PR). For `encode/httpx` with `--max 10`, we fetched 10 PRs but only 2 were merged — 80% of API calls were wasted on trajectories that `format_for_distillation()` filters out. Worse, the 2 merged PRs we got were low-quality (single commit, no review comments).\n\nThe fix: use GitHub's Search API to pre-qualify PRs before the expensive per-PR mining. One search request (1 API call) returns up to 100 PRs with enough metadata to filter on merge status, review count, comment count, and labels — all before spending 3+ calls per PR on commit/review/detail fetching.\n\n**API budget math:**\n- Search API: 30 req/min (separate rate limit bucket from core API)\n- 1 search request = up to 100 qualified PR candidates\n- Current approach: 100 PRs × 3+ calls = 300+ core API calls, most wasted\n- New approach: 1 search call + (qualified PRs × 3+ calls) = much less waste\n\n## Files to Modify\n\n| File | Action | What |\n|------|--------|------|\n| `libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_mining.py` | Modify | Add `search_quality_prs()`, refactor `mine_pr_diff_chains` to accept pre-filtered PR list |\n| `libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_mining.py` | Modify | Add tests for search_quality_prs and filtering |\n| `scripts/mine_github.py` | Modify | Add `--min-reviews`, `--min-commits`, `--exclude-labels` CLI flags |\n\n## Implementation\n\n### 1. Add `search_quality_prs()` to `d2l_mining.py`\n\nNew function that uses GitHub's Search API (`GET /search/issues`) to find high-quality PR candidates.\n\n```python\ndef search_quality_prs(\n    repo: str,\n    max_results: int = 100,\n    github_token: str | None = None,\n    min_review_comments: int = 1,\n    min_commits: int = 2,\n    exclude_labels: list[str] | None = None,\n) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n```\n\n**Search query construction:**\n```\nrepo:{repo} is:pr is:merged review:approved comments:>{min_review_comments}\n```\n\nGitHub Search qualifiers available (no extra API calls):\n- `is:pr is:merged` — only merged PRs (eliminates all \"failure\" waste)\n- `review:approved` — was reviewed and approved (has review cycle)\n- `comments:>N` — has discussion (proxy for self-correcting structure)\n- `-label:dependencies -label:documentation` — skip bot/docs PRs\n\n**What Search API returns per item (free metadata):**\n- `number`, `title`, `body`, `state`, `pull_request.merged_at`\n- `comments` count\n- `labels` array\n\n**What Search API does NOT return:**\n- Commit count (need 1 extra call to `GET /repos/{repo}/pulls/{number}` for `commits` field)\n- Review comment details\n- Commit diffs\n\n**Strategy for `min_commits` filter:** The search API doesn't expose commit count. Two options:\n- **Option A:** Fetch PR detail (`GET /repos/{repo}/pulls/{number}`) for each search result — 1 call per candidate to get `commits` count, then filter. Still cheaper than fetching all commits+details.\n- **Option B:** Skip the commit count filter at search time, apply it during mining (after fetching commits anyway). Simpler, slightly less efficient.\n\n**Recommended: Option A** — the PR detail endpoint returns `commits` count in a single call. We filter on it before the expensive commit-detail fetching. For 100 search results, that's 100 extra calls vs potentially 300+ saved.\n\n### 2. Add `exclude_labels` post-filter\n\nSearch API supports `-label:X` but it's fragile with special characters. Safer to post-filter the search results by checking the `labels` array. Default excludes:\n\n```python\n_DEFAULT_EXCLUDE_LABELS = {\"dependencies\", \"documentation\", \"docs\", \"chore\", \"ci\", \"bot\"}\n```\n\n### 3. Refactor `mine_pr_diff_chains()` to accept pre-filtered PRs\n\nAdd an optional `pr_numbers: list[int] | None` parameter. When provided, skip the list-PRs step and mine only those specific PRs:\n\n```python\ndef mine_pr_diff_chains(\n    repo: str,\n    max_prs: int = 100,\n    github_token: str | None = None,\n    pr_numbers: list[int] | None = None,  # NEW: pre-filtered PR numbers\n) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n```\n\nWhen `pr_numbers` is given:\n- Fetch each PR individually: `GET /repos/{repo}/pulls/{number}`\n- Then proceed with existing commits/reviews/detail logic\n- Skip the bulk list endpoint entirely\n\n### 4. Update CLI (`scripts/mine_github.py`)\n\nNew flags:\n```\n--quality          Enable quality pre-filtering via Search API (default: off for backward compat)\n--min-reviews N    Minimum review comments (default: 1)\n--min-commits N    Minimum commits (default: 2)\n--exclude-labels   Comma-separated labels to skip (default: dependencies,documentation,docs,chore,ci,bot)\n```\n\nWhen `--quality` is set:\n1. Call `search_quality_prs()` to get candidate PR numbers\n2. Pass those numbers to `mine_pr_diff_chains(pr_numbers=...)`\n3. Log filtering stats: \"Found X candidates from search, mining Y after filters\"\n\n### 5. ## Approved Plan:
# Quality-First GitHub Mining Filter

## Context

The current mining implementation fetches all closed PRs, then mines each one (3+ API calls per PR). For `encode/httpx` with `--max 10`, we fetched 10 PRs but only 2 were merged — 80% of API calls were wasted on trajectories that `format_for_distillation()` filters out. Worse, the 2 merged PRs we got were low-quality (single commit, no review comments).

The fix: use GitHub's Search API to pre-qualify PRs before the expensive per-PR mining. One search request (1 API call) returns up to 100 PRs with enough metadata to filter on merge status, review count, comment count, and labels — all before spending 3+ calls per PR on commit/review/detail fetching.

**API budget math:**
- Search API: 30 req/min (separate rate limit bucket from core API)
- 1 search request = up to 100 qualified PR candidates
- Current approach: 100 PRs × 3+ calls = 300+ core API calls, most wasted
- New approach: 1 search call + (qualified PRs × 3+ calls) = much less waste

## Files to Modify

| File | Action | What |
|------|--------|------|
| `libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_mining.py` | Modify | Add `search_quality_prs()`, refactor `mine_pr_diff_chains` to accept pre-filtered PR list |
| `libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_mining.py` | Modify | Add tests for search_quality_prs and filtering |
| `scripts/mine_github.py` | Modify | Add `--min-reviews`, `--min-commits`, `--exclude-labels` CLI flags |

## Implementation

### 1. Add `search_quality_prs()` to `d2l_mining.py`

New function that uses GitHub's Search API (`GET /search/issues`) to find high-quality PR candidates.

```python
def search_quality_prs(
    repo: str,
    max_results: int = 100,
    github_token: str | None = None,
    min_review_comments: int = 1,
    min_commits: int = 2,
    exclude_labels: list[str] | None = None,
) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
```

**Search query construction:**
```
repo:{repo} is:pr is:merged review:approved comments:>{min_review_comments}
```

GitHub Search qualifiers available (no extra API calls):
- `is:pr is:merged` — only merged PRs (eliminates all "failure" waste)
- `review:approved` — was reviewed and approved (has review cycle)
- `comments:>N` — has discussion (proxy for self-correcting structure)
- `-label:dependencies -label:documentation` — skip bot/docs PRs

**What Search API returns per item (free metadata):**
- `number`, `title`, `body`, `state`, `pull_request.merged_at`
- `comments` count
- `labels` array

**What Search API does NOT return:**
- Commit count (need 1 extra call to `GET /repos/{repo}/pulls/{number}` for `commits` field)
- Review comment details
- Commit diffs

**Strategy for `min_commits` filter:** The search API doesn't expose commit count. Two options:
- **Option A:** Fetch PR detail (`GET /repos/{repo}/pulls/{number}`) for each search result — 1 call per candidate to get `commits` count, then filter. Still cheaper than fetching all commits+details.
- **Option B:** Skip the commit count filter at search time, apply it during mining (after fetching commits anyway). Simpler, slightly less efficient.

**Recommended: Option A** — the PR detail endpoint returns `commits` count in a single call. We filter on it before the expensive commit-detail fetching. For 100 search results, that's 100 extra calls vs potentially 300+ saved.

### 2. Add `exclude_labels` post-filter

Search API supports `-label:X` but it's fragile with special characters. Safer to post-filter the search results by checking the `labels` array. Default excludes:

```python
_DEFAULT_EXCLUDE_LABELS = {"dependencies", "documentation", "docs", "chore", "ci", "bot"}
```

### 3. Refactor `mine_pr_diff_chains()` to accept pre-filtered PRs

Add an optional `pr_numbers: list[int] | None` parameter. When provided, skip the list-PRs step and mine only those specific PRs:

```python
def mine_pr_diff_chains(
    repo: str,
    max_prs: int = 100,
    github_token: str | None = None,
    pr_numbers: list[int] | None = None,  # NEW: pre-filtered PR numbers
) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
```

When `pr_numbers` is given:
- Fetch each PR individually: `GET /repos/{repo}/pulls/{number}`
- Then proceed with existing commits/reviews/detail logic
- Skip the bulk list endpoint entirely

### 4. Update CLI (`scripts/mine_github.py`)

New flags:
```
--quality          Enable quality pre-filtering via Search API (default: off for backward compat)
--min-reviews N    Minimum review comments (default: 1)
--min-commits N    Minimum commits (default: 2)
--exclude-labels   Comma-separated labels to skip (default: dependencies,documentation,docs,chore,ci,bot)
```

When `--quality` is set:
1. Call `search_quality_prs()` to get candidate PR numbers
2. Pass those numbers to `mine_pr_diff_chains(pr_numbers=...)`
3. Log filtering stats: "Found X candidates from search, mining Y after filters"

### 5. Tests

Add to `test_d2l_mining.py`:

**TestSearchQualityPrs:**
- `test_search_returns_merged_prs_only` — mock search response, verify all results have merged_at
- `test_excludes_labeled_prs` — PR with "dependencies" label excluded
- `test_min_commits_filter` — PR with 1 commit filtered out when min_commits=2
- `test_empty_search_returns_empty` — no results -> empty list
- `test_mine_with_pr_numbers` — mine_pr_diff_chains with explicit pr_numbers fetches individually

## API Call Comparison

**Mining 100 PRs from a repo (current):**
```
1 list-PRs call
+ 100 × (1 commits-list + 1 reviews-list + N commit-details)
= ~400+ calls, most wasted on unmerged PRs
```

**Mining 100 quality PRs (new):**
```
1 search call (returns up to 100 merged+reviewed candidates)
+ 100 × 1 PR-detail call (to check commit count)
→ filter to ~30-50 candidates
+ 30-50 × (1 commits-list + 1 reviews-list + N commit-details)
= ~200 calls, all on quality PRs
```

50% fewer API calls, 100% of results are training-quality.

## Verification

1. `uv run pytest libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_mining.py -v` — all tests pass
2. `uv run ruff check libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_mining.py`
3. E2E: `uv run python scripts/mine_github.py --repo encode/httpx --mode prs --max 10 --quality --normalize -o /tmp/test.json` — should return only multi-commit, reviewed, merged PRs

```

{
  "command": "gh pr create --title \"feat: GitHub training data mining pipeline\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n\n- Implements `mine_pr_diff_chains()` and `mine_issue_commit_chains()` — previously stubbed with `NotImplementedError` — to mine GitHub repositories for coding trajectory data suitable for Doc-to-LoRA hypernetwork training\n- Adds `search_quality_prs()` using GitHub Search API to pre-filter for merged, reviewed, multi-commit PRs before expensive per-PR mining (50% fewer API calls)\n- Adds `normalize_mined_trajectory()` bridge to convert mining output to distillation-compatible format (handles asymmetric PR/issue outcome semantics)\n- Adds `scripts/mine_github.py` CLI with `--quality`, `--min-reviews`, `--min-commits`, `--exclude-labels` flags\n- Adds `GitHubClient` helper with auth, pagination, and rate-limit retry (shared between `get()` and `get_paginated()`)\n\n## Key design decisions\n\n- **Filter first, mine second**: GitHub Search API pre-qualifies PRs (1 call = 100 candidates) before the expensive per-PR commit/review fetching\n- **PR \"merged\" = success, Issue \"closed\" = success**: Asymmetric outcome normalization — closed unmerged PRs are failures, but closed issues are successes\n- **Step descriptions enriched with `[Commit]`/`[Review]` prefixes**: These become the `activation_text` that the hypernetwork processes for adapter generation\n- **Final commit patch = answer**: The last commit's diff becomes `canonical_solution` in `teacher_text`, preserving iterative structure in activation context\n\n## Test plan\n\n- [ ] 25 new unit tests across 4 test files (GitHubClient, mining functions, normalization, quality search)\n- [ ] Full test suite passes (386 tests)\n- [ ] E2E verified: `mine_github.py --repo encode/httpx --quality --normalize` produces valid training JSONL through `d2l_prep`\n- [ ] ruff check + mypy clean on all new code\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1",
  "description": "Create pull request",
  "timeout": 30000
}

[open] PR #17: feat: GitHub training data mining pipeline
  ET-NoahDolev
  OPEN | [ok]
  Checks: 8/9 passed
  https://github.com/ElixirTrials/rune/pull/17

  ## Summary
  
  - Implements `mine_pr_diff_chains()` and `mine_issue_commit_chains()` — previously stubbed with `NotImplementedError` — to mine GitHub repositories for coding trajectory data suitable for Doc-to-LoRA hypernetwork training
  - Adds `search_quality_prs()` using GitHub Search API to pre-filter for merged, reviewed, multi-commit PRs before expensive per-PR mining (50% fewer API calls)
  - Adds `normalize_mined_trajectory()` bridge to convert mining output to distillation-compatible format (handles asymmetric PR/issue outcome semantics)
  - Adds `scripts/mine_github.py` CLI with `--quality`, `--min-reviews`, `--min-commits`, `--exclude-labels` flags
  - Adds `GitHubClient` helper with auth, pagination, and rate-limit retry (shared between `get()` and `get_paginated()`)
  
  ## Key design decisions
  
  - **Filter first, mine second**: GitHub Search API pre-qualifies PRs (1 call = 100 candidates) before the expensive per-PR commit/review fetching
  - **PR "merged" = success, Issue "closed" = success**: Asymmetric outcome normalization — closed unmerged PRs are failures, but closed issues are successes
  - **Step descriptions enriched with `[Commit]`/`[Review]` prefixes**: These become the `activation_text` that the hypernetwork processes for adapter generation
  - **Final commit patch = answer**: The last commit's diff becomes `canonical_solution` in `teacher_text`, preserving iterative structure in activation context
  
  ## Test plan
  
  - [ ] 25 new unit tests across 4 test files (GitHubClient, mining functions, normalization, quality search)
  - [ ] Full test suite passes (386 tests)
  - [ ] E2E verified: `mine_github.py --repo encode/httpx --quality --normalize` produces valid training JSONL through `d2l_prep`
  - [ ] ruff check + mypy clean on all new code
  
  🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
```

6\. Pr steps misordered <code>🐞 Bug</code> <code>≡ Correctness</code>

<pre>
mine_pr_diff_chains() claims commit diffs and review comments are returned in chronological order,
but it appends all commits first and all review comments afterward, so review feedback cannot appear
between the commits it influenced. This produces trajectories whose step order contradicts the
function contract and distorts the activation context built from step descriptions.
</pre>

### Issue description
`mine_pr_diff_chains()` advertises chronological steps but currently appends commit steps and review-comment steps in two separate phases, preventing true chronological interleaving.

### Issue Context
`format_for_distillation()` builds the activation context by joining step `description` values in list order, so step ordering is semantically important.

### Fix Focus Areas
- libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_mining.py[172-208]
- libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_data.py[304-316]

### Implementation notes
- Capture a timestamp for each commit step (e.g., from the commit list payload if present, or from commit detail) and for each review comment (e.g., `created_at`).
- Build a unified list of steps with a `ts` field and `sort(key=lambda s: s["ts"])` (ascending), then drop `ts` before returning.
- Keep the existing output schema (`type`, `description`, `content`) so `normalize_mined_trajectory()` keeps working.
```

126	    """Extract PR diff chains from a GitHub repository.

128	    Each chain represents an iterative coding session: initial commit ->

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_mining.py",
  "old_string": "        steps: list[dict[str, str]] = []\n\n        for commit in commits:\n            sha = commit[\"sha\"]\n            msg = commit[\"commit\"][\"message\"]\n            detail = client.get(f\"/repos/{repo}/commits/{sha}\")\n            files = detail.get(\"files\", [])\n            patches = []\n            for f in files:\n                patch = f.get(\"patch\", \"\")\n                if patch:\n                    patches.append(f\"--- {f['filename']} ---\\n{patch}\")\n            steps.append(\n                {\n                    \"type\": \"commit\",\n                    \"description\": msg,\n                    \"content\": \"\\n\".join(patches),\n                }\n            )\n\n        for comment in reviews:\n            steps.append(\n                {\n                    \"type\": \"review\",\n                    \"description\": \"Review comment\",\n                    \"content\": comment.get(\"body\", \"\"),\n                }\n            )",
  "new_string": "        # Build timestamped steps for chronological interleaving\n        timed_steps: list[tuple[str, dict[str, str]]] = []\n\n        for commit in commits:\n            sha = commit[\"sha\"]\n            msg = commit[\"commit\"][\"message\"]\n            ts = commit[\"commit\"].get(\"committer\", {}).get(\"date\", \"\")\n            detail = client.get(f\"/repos/{repo}/commits/{sha}\")\n            files = detail.get(\"files\", [])\n            patches = []\n            for f in files:\n                patch = f.get(\"patch\", \"\")\n                if patch:\n                    patches.append(f\"--- {f['filename']} ---\\n{patch}\")\n            timed_steps.append((\n                ts,\n                {\n                    \"type\": \"commit\",\n                    \"description\": msg,\n                    \"content\": \"\\n\".join(patches),\n                },\n            ))\n\n        for comment in reviews:\n            ts = comment.get(\"created_at\", \"\")\n            timed_steps.append((\n                ts,\n                {\n                    \"type\": \"review\",\n                    \"description\": \"Review comment\",\n                    \"content\": comment.get(\"body\", \"\"),\n                },\n            ))\n\n        # Sort by timestamp so commits and reviews interleave chronologically\n        timed_steps.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])\n        steps = [step for _, step in timed_steps]"
}

\"--output\",\n+        required=True,\n+        type=Path,\n+        metavar=\"FILE\",\n+        help=\"Output JSON file path.\",\n+    )\n+    parser.add_argument(\n+        \"--normalize\",\n+
